Based on the search results, here's what I found about "ragcode MCP server npm claude code":

## RagCode MCP Server

RagCode is a Model Context Protocol (MCP) server that instantly makes your project AI-ready and enables AI assistants like GitHub Copilot, Cursor, Windsurf, and Claude to understand your entire codebase through semantic vector search.

### Key Features

RagCode provides deep AST multi-language support (Go, PHP/Laravel/WP, JS/TS/React, Python) and runs entirely on your local infrastructure with local AI models using Ollama, local vector database using Qdrant, zero cloud dependencies, and no API costs.

### Installation

Built with the official Model Context Protocol Go SDK, RagCode provides 9 powerful tools to index, search, and analyze code. The installation is straightforward with platform-specific downloads available.

### How It Works

RagCode runs entirely on your local infrastructure with no external API calls, no data transmission, and works fully offline after the initial model download.

### Integration with Claude Code

Claude Code can connect to hundreds of external tools and data sources through the Model Context Protocol (MCP), an open source standard for AI-tool integrations, giving Claude Code access to your tools, databases, and APIs.

The RagCode MCP server can be used with Claude Code to provide semantic code search and navigation capabilities directly within your development workflow.

Based on the search results, here's what I found about token-optimizer-mcp:

## Overview

Token Optimizer is a Model Context Protocol (MCP) server designed to dramatically cut down on token consumption when interacting with Claude Code and Claude Desktop.

## Key Features

It achieves this by intelligently combining Brotli compression, a persistent SQLite-based caching system, and accurate token tracking. More specifically, it's a high-performance MCP server that minimizes LLM token usage and API costs via structural code analysis, precision chunking, and semantic vector search.

## Functionality

The server provides several optimization tools:
- Smart File Chunking: Never send a 10,000 line file again with hard caps on line ranges.
- Structural Summarization: Extract classes, imports, and function signatures to give the LLM a topological map of a file without the payload of raw code.
- Trimmed Search: Recursive codebase searching that returns only file paths and micro-snippets.
- Delta Diffing: Returns only the modified lines (git diff), ensuring unmodified code is never re-processed.

## Installation & Setup

The server is configured entirely via environment variables; no external data files required. To hook the optimizer up to your agent (e.g., Claude Desktop, Cursor, or Codex, Antigravity), simply define the environment variables and point the command to token-optimizer-mcp.

## Repository

The GitHub repository is available at https://github.com/ooples/token-optimizer-mcp, and the server is also listed on MCP marketplaces like LobeHub and MCPMarket.

### RagCode MCP - Make Your Codebase AI-Ready > 💻 IDE Integration

## 💻 IDE Integration

[](#-ide-integration)

RagCode works with all major AI-powered IDEs:

| IDE | Status | Setup |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Windsurf** | ✅ Auto-configured | Just install |
| **Cursor** | ✅ Auto-configured | Just install |
| **VS Code + Copilot** | ✅ Auto-configured | Requires VS Code 1.95+ |
| **Claude Desktop** | ✅ Auto-configured | Just install |
| **Antigravity** | ✅ Auto-configured | Just install |

### GitHub Actions CI/CD Documentation > Setup Instructions > 1\. Initial Setup

### 1\. Initial Setup

[](#1-initial-setup)

1.  **Clone the repository**
    
    ```shell
    git clone https://github.com/ooples/token-optimizer-mcp.git
    cd token-optimizer-mcp
    ```
    
2.  **Install dependencies**
    
    ```shell
    npm install
    ```
    
3.  **Install semantic-release and commitlint globally** (optional)
    
    ```shell
    npm install -g semantic-release @commitlint/cli
    ```

### GitHub Actions CI/CD Documentation > Secrets and Variables > Setting Up Notifications > Discord

#### Discord

[](#discord)

1.  Go to your Discord server settings
2.  Navigate to "Integrations" → "Webhooks"
3.  Create a new webhook
4.  Copy the webhook URL
5.  Add to GitHub Variables as `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL`

{
  "name": "ragcode",
  "version": "1.1.17",
  "description": "Semantic code navigation using RAG (Retrieval-Augmented Generation) with multi-language support (Go, PHP, Python, JavaScript). Compatible with Windsurf, Cursor, Antigravity, Claude Desktop, and VS Code + GitHub Copilot (agent mode).",
  "homepage": "https://github.com/doITmagic/rag-code-mcp",
  "license": "MIT",
  "runtime": "binary",
  "command": "rag-code-mcp",
  "capabilities": {
    "tools": true,
    "resources": true,
    "prompts": false,
    "sampling": false
  },
  "dependencies": {
    "ollama": {
      "required": true,
      "url": "https://ollama.ai",
      "description": "Local LLM for embeddings and chat"
    },
    "qdrant": {
      "required": true,
      "url": "https://qdrant.tech",
      "description": "Vector database for code indexing"
    }
  },
  "tools": [
    {
      "name": "search_code",
      "description": "Semantic search across indexed codebase"
    },
    {
      "name": "hybrid_search",
      "description": "Combined semantic and lexical search"
    },
    {
      "name": "get_function_details",
      "description": "Get detailed information about a function or method"
    },
    {
      "name": "find_type_definition",
      "description": "Find the definition of a struct or interface"
    },
    {
      "name": "get_code_context",
      "description": "Extract code context for specific line ranges"
    },
    {
      "name": "list_package_exports",
      "description": "List exported symbols from a package"
    },
    {
      "name": "find_implementations",
      "description": "Find implementations and usages of a symbol"
    },
    {
      "name": "search_docs",
      "description": "Search indexed documentation"
    },
    {
      "name": "index_workspace",
      "description": "Manually trigger indexing of a workspace (auto-indexing is enabled by default)"
    }
  ],
  "resources": [
    {
      "name": "project-readme",
      "description": "Project README file"
    },
    {
      "name": "ragcode-readme",
      "description": "MCP RagCode guide"
    },
    {
      "name": "config",
      "description": "Server configuration file"
    }
  ],
  "configuration": {
    "env": {
      "OLLAMA_BASE_URL": {
        "description": "Ollama server URL",
        "default": "http://localhost:11434"
      },
      "OLLAMA_MODEL": {
        "description": "Ollama chat model",
        "default": "phi3:medium"
      },
      "OLLAMA_EMBED": {
        "description": "Ollama embedding model",
        "default": "mxbai-embed-large"
      },
      "QDRANT_URL": {
        "description": "Qdrant server URL",
        "default": "http://localhost:6333"
      },
      "QDRANT_COLLECTION": {
        "description": "Qdrant collection name for code index",
        "default": "do-ai-code"
      },
      "MCP_LOG_LEVEL": {
        "description": "Logging level (debug, info, warn, error)",
        "default": "info"
      },
      "MCP_LOG_FILE": {
        "description": "Path to log file for debugging (optional)",
        "default": ""
      }

{
  "name": "io.github.ooples/token-optimizer-mcp",
  "version": "0.2.0",
  "description": "Intelligent token optimization for Claude Code - achieving 95%+ token reduction through caching, compression, and smart tool intelligence",
  "author": {
    "name": "ooples",
    "url": "https://github.com/ooples"
  },
  "sourceRepository": "https://github.com/ooples/token-optimizer-mcp",
  "license": "MIT",
  "runtime": "node",
  "homepage": "https://github.com/ooples/token-optimizer-mcp#readme",
  "capabilities": {
    "tools": {},
    "resources": {}
  },
  "installation": {
    "npm": {
      "package": "@ooples/token-optimizer-mcp",
      "command": "npx @ooples/token-optimizer-mcp"
    }
  },
  "config": {
    "mcpServers": {
      "token-optimizer": {
        "command": "node",
        "args": [
          "./dist/server/index.js"
        ],
        "env": {
          "TOKEN_OPTIMIZER_CACHE_DIR": "${HOME}/.token-optimizer-cache"
        }
      }
    }
  }
}

# RagCode MCP - Make Your Codebase AI-Ready

> **The privacy-first MCP server that transforms any repository into an AI-ready codebase with semantic search and RAG.**

<!-- AI Agents: Read llms.txt for a summarized view of this project -->

RagCode is a **Model Context Protocol (MCP) server** that instantly makes your project **AI-ready**. It enables AI assistants like **GitHub Copilot**, **Cursor**, **Windsurf**, and **Claude** to understand your entire codebase through **semantic vector search**, bridging the gap between your code and Large Language Models (LLMs).

Built with the official [Model Context Protocol Go SDK](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/go-sdk), RagCode provides **9 powerful tools** to index, search, and analyze code, making it the ultimate solution for **AI-ready software development**.

## ⚡ One-Command Installation

**No Go, no build tools, no configuration needed. Just Docker.**

### Linux (amd64)
```bash
curl -fsSL https://github.com/doITmagic/rag-code-mcp/releases/latest/download/rag-code-mcp_linux_amd64.tar.gz | tar xz && ./ragcode-installer -ollama=docker -qdrant=docker
```

### macOS (Apple Silicon / Intel)
```bash
# Apple Silicon (M1/M2/M3)
curl -fsSL https://github.com/doITmagic/rag-code-mcp/releases/latest/download/rag-code-mcp_darwin_arm64.tar.gz | tar xz && ./ragcode-installer -ollama=docker -qdrant=docker

# Intel Macs
curl -fsSL https://github.com/doITmagic/rag-code-mcp/releases/latest/download/rag-code-mcp_darwin_amd64.tar.gz | tar xz && ./ragcode-installer -ollama=docker -qdrant=docker
```

### Windows (PowerShell)
```powershell
Invoke-WebRequest -Uri "https://github.com/doITmagic/rag-code-mcp/releases/latest/download/rag-code-mcp_windows_amd64.zip" -OutFile "ragcode.zip"; Expand-Archive ragcode.zip -DestinationPath . -Force; .\ragcode-installer.exe -ollama=docker -qdrant=docker
```

# Token Optimizer MCP

> Intelligent token optimization through caching, compression, and smart tooling for Claude Code and Claude Desktop

## Overview

Token Optimizer MCP is a Model Context Protocol (MCP) server that reduces context window usage by 60-90% through intelligent caching, compression, and smart tool replacements. By storing compressed content externally in SQLite and providing optimized alternatives to standard tools, the server helps you maximize your available context window.

**Production Results**: 60-90% token reduction across 38,000+ operations in real-world usage.

## Key Features

- **Smart Tool Replacements**: Automatic optimization for Read, Grep, Glob, and more
- **Context Window Optimization**: Store content externally to free up context space
- **High Compression**: Brotli compression (2-4x typical, up to 82x for repetitive content)
- **Persistent Caching**: SQLite-based cache that persists across sessions
- **Accurate Token Counting**: Uses tiktoken for precise token measurements
- **61 Specialized Tools**: File operations, API caching, database optimization, monitoring, and more
- **Zero External Dependencies**: Completely offline operation
- **Production Ready**: Built with TypeScript for reliability

## Installation

### Quick Install (Recommended)

#### Windows

```powershell
# Run PowerShell as Administrator, then:
Set-ExecutionPolicy -ExecutionPolicy RemoteSigned -Scope CurrentUser

# Install globally (hooks install automatically!)
npm install -g @ooples/token-optimizer-mcp
```

#### macOS / Linux

```bash
# Install globally (hooks install automatically!)
npm install -g @ooples/token-optimizer-mcp
```

That's it! The postinstall script will automatically:
1. ✅ Install token-optimizer-mcp globally via npm
2. ✅ Auto-detect and configure all installed AI tools (Claude Desktop, Cursor, Cline, etc.)
3. ✅ Set up automatic token optimization on every tool call
4. ✅ Configure workspace trust and execution permissions

**Result**: 60-90% token reduction across all operations!

**Note**: If automatic installation is skipped (e.g., in CI environments), you can manually run the installer:
- Windows: `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File install-hooks.ps1`
- macOS/Linux: `bash install-hooks.sh`

### Manual Configuration

For detailed platform-specific installation instructions, see [docs/HOOKS-INSTALLATION.md](./docs/HOOKS-INSTALLATION.md).

## Available Tools (65 Total)

### Core Caching & Optimization (8 tools)

<details>
<summary>Click to expand</summary>

- **optimize_text** - Compress and cache text (primary tool for token reduction)
- **get_cached** - Retrieve previously cached text
- **compress_text** - Compress text using Brotli
- **decompress_text** - Decompress Brotli-compressed text
- **count_tokens** - Count tokens using tiktoken (GPT-4 tokenizer)
- **analyze_optimization** - Analyze text and get optimization recommendations
- **get_cache_stats** - View cache hit rates and compression ratios
- **clear_cache** - Clear all cached data

# 🖥️ IDE Setup Guide

Manual configuration for RagCode MCP in various IDEs and AI assistants.

> **Note:** The `ragcode-installer` automatically configures most IDEs. Use this guide only if you need manual setup or installed an IDE after running the installer.

---

## 📍 Configuration File Locations

| IDE | Config File Path |
|-----|------------------|
| **Windsurf** | `~/.codeium/windsurf/mcp_config.json` |
| **Cursor** | `~/.cursor/mcp.config.json` |
| **Antigravity** | `~/.gemini/antigravity/mcp_config.json` |
| **Claude Desktop (Linux)** | `~/.config/Claude/mcp-servers.json` |
| **Claude Desktop (macOS)** | `~/Library/Application Support/Claude/mcp-servers.json` |
| **Claude Desktop (Windows)** | `%APPDATA%\Claude\mcp-servers.json` |
| **VS Code + Copilot** | `~/.config/Code/User/globalStorage/mcp-servers.json` |

---

## 🔧 Standard Configuration

Add this to your IDE's MCP configuration file (create the file if it doesn't exist):

### Linux / macOS

```json
{
  "mcpServers": {
    "ragcode": {
      "command": "/home/YOUR_USERNAME/.local/share/ragcode/bin/rag-code-mcp",
      "args": [],
      "env": {
        "OLLAMA_BASE_URL": "http://localhost:11434",
        "OLLAMA_MODEL": "phi3:medium",
        "OLLAMA_EMBED": "mxbai-embed-large",
        "QDRANT_URL": "http://localhost:6333"
      }
    }
  }
}
```

> **Important:** Replace `YOUR_USERNAME` with your actual system username.
> 
> On macOS, the path is typically `/Users/YOUR_USERNAME/.local/share/ragcode/bin/rag-code-mcp`

### Windows (Native)

```json
{
  "mcpServers": {
    "ragcode": {
      "command": "C:\\Users\\YOUR_USERNAME\\.local\\share\\ragcode\\bin\\rag-code-mcp.exe",
      "args": [],
      "env": {
        "OLLAMA_BASE_URL": "http://localhost:11434",
        "OLLAMA_MODEL": "phi3:medium",
        "OLLAMA_EMBED": "mxbai-embed-large",
        "QDRANT_URL": "http://localhost:6333"
      }
    }
  }
}
```

### Windows with WSL

If you run RagCode inside WSL but use Windows IDEs:

```json
{
  "mcpServers": {
    "ragcode": {
      "command": "wsl.exe",
      "args": ["-e", "/home/YOUR_USERNAME/.local/share/ragcode/bin/rag-code-mcp"],
      "env": {
        "OLLAMA_BASE_URL": "http://localhost:11434",
        "OLLAMA_MODEL": "phi3:medium",
        "OLLAMA_EMBED": "mxbai-embed-large",
        "QDRANT_URL": "http://localhost:6333"
      },
      "disabled": false
    }
  }
}
```

> **Note:** The `localhost` URLs work because WSL2 shares network ports with Windows.

---

Both configs are updated. Here's what I did:

**token-optimizer-mcp:**
- Installed globally via `npm install -g @ooples/token-optimizer-mcp`
- Added to `.mcp.json` and `enabledMcpjsonServers`

**ragcode:**
- Binary already existed at `~/.local/share/ragcode/bin/rag-code-mcp` (v1.1.21)
- Ollama models already available (`mxbai-embed-large`, `llama3.2`)
- Started Qdrant Docker container (`qdrant/qdrant` on port 6333)
- Added to `.mcp.json` and `enabledMcpjsonServers`
- Configured to use your existing `llama3.2` model (instead of default `phi3:medium`)

**To connect them:** Run `/mcp` again to reconnect, or restart the Claude Code session. The servers should now be discoverable.

Note: Qdrant runs as a Docker container (`qdrant`) — you'll need Docker running for ragcode to work. The `.mcp.json` change will show as a git diff since it's tracked; you may want to `git stash` it or add it to `.gitignore` if you don't want it committed.

## ragcode (9 tools)

| `search_code` | Semantic search across indexed codebase |

| `find_type_definition` | Find struct/interface definitions |
| `get_code_context` | Extract code context for specific line ranges |
| `list_package_exports` | List exported symbols from a package |
| `find_implementations` | Find implementations and usages of a symbol |
| `search_docs` | Search indexed documentation |
| `index_workspace` | Manually trigger workspace indexing |

> AGENT

The `optimize_session` tool is hitting a JSON parsing error — it appears to be failing to auto-detect or parse the session JSONL file. This is likely a known issue with `token-optimizer-mcp` where it can't locate or parse Claude Code's session log format.

The other token-optimizer tools (smart_read, smart_grep, compress_text, etc.) should work fine — the session optimizer specifically depends on reading Claude Code's internal session logs, which may have changed format. You can still use the core optimization tools like `optimize_text` and `compress_text` manually.

> AGENT

Here are 10 open-source freemium SaaS products with standout websites worth studying:

| # | Product | URL | What They Nail |
|---|---------|-----|----------------|
| 1 | **Supabase** | supabase.com | Developer-first hero, live code snippets, dark theme, pricing clarity |
| 2 | **PostHog** | posthog.com | Personality-driven brand, quirky illustrations, transparent pricing, hedgehog mascot |
| 3 | **Cal.com** | cal.com | Clean minimalism, strong open-source badge, instant demo embed |
| 4 | **Infisical** | infisical.com | Trust signals (SOC2/GDPR badges), animated terminal demos, enterprise vs OSS split |
| 5 | **Plane** | plane.so | Polished product screenshots, feature comparison tables, community-first messaging |
| 6 | **n8n** | n8n.io | Interactive workflow builder preview, use-case driven navigation, template gallery |
| 7 | **Appwrite** | appwrite.io | SDK-first landing, multi-language code tabs, gradient-heavy modern aesthetic |
| 8 | **Twenty** | twenty.com | Ultra-clean CRM product shots, bold "open-source alternative to Salesforce" positioning |
| 9 | **Dub** | dub.co | Conversion-focused hero, live stats counter, sleek micro-interactions |
| 10 | **Documenso** | documenso.com | Trust-centered copy, side-by-side OSS vs proprietary comparison, signing demo |

**Common patterns across all of them:**
- Dark/gradient hero sections with animated product previews
- "Self-host or Cloud" toggle prominently placed
- GitHub stars badge as social proof
- Transparent pricing with generous free tier highlighted
- Community section (Discord/GitHub contributors)
- "Used by" logo bars for credibility

Want me to deep-dive into any of these for specific design patterns (pricing pages, hero sections, onboarding flows)?

Here is the structural breakdown of Infisical's website.

---

## Infisical Website Structural Breakdown

### Landing Page (infisical.com)

**1. Navigation Bar** -- Logo, GitHub stars badge, Docs, Careers, Blog, Pricing, "Get a Demo" (outlined) + "Sign Up" (filled). Two CTAs in the nav is notable.

**2. Hero Section** -- Headline: "Secrets, certificates, and access management on autopilot." Subhead explains the all-in-one platform value prop in one sentence. Dual CTAs: "Get Started" (primary) + "Get a Demo" (secondary). Green-themed background graphic.

**3. Logo Bar (Social Proof)** -- Scrolling row of enterprise logos: VW, Hugging Face, LG, NVIDIA, UPS, Marsh McLennan, SolarWinds, Hinge Health, HeyGen, Fortescue, Writer, Lucid. Heavy on recognizable Fortune 500 names.

**4. Product Feature Tabs** -- Four tabbed product lines, each with its own page-section: **Secrets Management**, **Certificate Manager**, **Privileged Access Management (PAM)**, **Secret Scanning**. Each tab has a short description (2 sentences), "Learn more" + "Try it out" CTAs, and a product screenshot.

**5. Vanity Metrics** -- "500 million+ secrets secured every day" -- single bold stat used as a section divider.

**6. Sub-Feature Cards (Governance)** -- Header: "Enterprise-level governance." Cards for: Audit Logs, Approval Workflows, Temporary Access, Dynamic Secrets & Secret Rotation. Each has a heading, 1-2 sentence description, screenshot, and "Learn more" link.

**7. Integrations Grid** -- Visual grid of 50+ integration logos (AWS, Vercel, GitHub Actions, GitLab, Jenkins, Kubernetes, Terraform, Docker, etc.) linking to docs.

**8. Trust/Compliance Section** -- Three items: "Self-hostable" (deploy on your infra or use Cloud), "Compliant" (SOC 2, HIPAA, FIPS 140-3, continuous pentesting), plus community stats: 3,000+ community members, 12,000+ organizations, 99.99% uptime SLA.

**9. Bottom CTA** -- "Starting with Infisical is simple, fast, and free." with "Get Started" button.

**10. Footer** -- Standard columns: Product, Resources, Legal, Community (Slack, Contributing).

### Pricing Page (infisical.com/pricing)

**Headline:** "Cheaper than a security breach." -- Fear-based hook, short and punchy.

**Product Line Selector:** Tabs for Secrets Manager, Certificate Manager, PAM, Secret Scanning -- pricing is per product line, not a single bundled price.

**Three Tiers:**

| | Free | Pro | Enterprise |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Price** | $0/mo | $18/mo per identity | Custom |
| **CTA** | "Get Started" | "Start Free Trial" | "Get a Demo" / "Contact Us" |
| **Limits** | 5 identities, 3 projects, 3 envs, 10 integrations | Unlimited identities/projects, 12 envs, 50 integrations | Unlimited everything |
| **Key adds** | Core platform, K8s operator, agent, webhooks, 2FA, self-hosting, all integrations | Versioning, PITR, RBAC, rotation, SAML SSO, IP allowlisting, 90-day audit logs | Dedicated infra, SCIM, LDAP, dynamic secrets, AI advisor, approval workflows, KMIP, HSM, custom SLA, dedicated support |

**Detailed Comparison Table** -- Expandable grid comparing every feature across tiers with checkmarks and limits.

**FAQ Section** -- Addresses: "What is Infisical?", subscription cancellation, free-to-pro upgrade path.

### Copy Patterns and Tone

- **Concise and direct** -- Headlines are under 10 words. Descriptions are 1-2 sentences max.
- **Action-oriented verbs** -- "Centralize," "Enforce," "Track," "Deploy," "Grant."
- **Fear + simplicity** -- Pricing headline uses fear ("security breach"), bottom CTA uses ease ("simple, fast, and free").
- **Developer-native language** -- References to runtime delivery, Kubernetes, CI/CD, not marketing fluff.

### Open Source vs Cloud Positioning

Self-hosting and Cloud are presented as equal deployment options within every tier (including Free). There is no separate "open source" tier -- the open-source nature is a trust signal, not a product boundary. The GitHub stars badge in the nav is the only OSS callout. The competitive comparison page ("Infisical vs Vault") handles differentiation from HashiCorp.

### Key Takeaways for Template Use

- Per-product-line pricing (not bundled) lets them expand the product surface without complicating the core pricing story.
- Dual CTA pattern ("Get Started" + "Get a Demo") appears in nav, hero, and bottom -- consistent throughout.
- Social proof is layered: logos first, then vanity metric, then compliance badges, then community stats.
- Free tier is generous enough to be useful (self-hosting included), creating a natural upgrade funnel to Pro via limits (identities, projects, environments).

## Comprehensive Raven Project Summary

I've completed a thorough exploration of the Raven codebase. Here's everything you need to know for building a marketing/landing page:

---

## What is Raven?

**Raven** is an open-source, self-hostable **multi-tenant knowledge base platform** with AI-powered chat, voice, and WhatsApp capabilities. It's designed for teams that want full data sovereignty and zero vendor lock-in. The platform lets organizations ingest documents and web content, then query them through AI-powered channels using a hybrid retrieval system (vector search + BM25 full-text search).

**Key positioning**: "The AI Brain for Your Entire Team" — open-source with MIT license (or enterprise edition available).

---

## Core Features

1. **AI-Powered Chat** — Semantic search + RAG over documents with cited, accurate answers
2. **Voice Agent** — Real-time voice calls via LiveKit WebRTC (hands-free knowledge access)
3. **WhatsApp Integration** — Query knowledge base via WhatsApp Business API (zero friction)
4. **Hybrid Vector Search** — pgvector (cosine similarity) + BM25 (full-text) combined with Reciprocal Rank Fusion
5. **Multi-Tenant Architecture** — Full data isolation per tenant via PostgreSQL RLS and Keycloak IAM
6. **BYOK (Bring Your Own Keys)** — Anthropic Claude, OpenAI, Cohere, or self-hosted LLMs (zero lock-in)
7. **Self-Hostable** — Single Docker Compose deployment, no external SaaS dependencies
8. **Edge-Deployable** — 25MB ARM64 Go binary; run API on Raspberry Pi with remote AI worker

---

## Tech Stack

| Layer | Technology | Purpose |
|-------|-----------|---------|
| **API Server** | Go 1.26+ + Gin | REST API, JWT validation, tenant routing, SSE streaming |
| **AI Worker** | Python 3.12+ + gRPC | RAG queries, embeddings, document parsing, web scraping |
| **Database** | PostgreSQL 18 + pgvector + ParadeDB | Relational data, vector search, BM25 |
| **Frontend** | Vue.js 3 + Tailwind CSS | Admin dashboard (SPA, mobile-first, PWA-capable) |
| **Chatbot Widget** | Web Component | Embeddable `<raven-chat>` element |
| **Auth** | Keycloak + OIDC/OAuth2 | User management, multi-tenant realms |
| **Job Queue** | Valkey (Redis fork) | Async document processing, caching, rate limiting |
| **Object Storage** | SeaweedFS | S3-compatible file storage (Apache 2.0) |
| **Voice** | LiveKit Server + Agents | WebRTC SFU, STT/LLM/TTS voice pipeline |
| **Reverse Proxy** | Traefik | Auto-TLS, routing, security headers |
| **Payments** | Hyperswitch + Razorpay | Payment orchestration (if billing enabled) |
| **Observability** | OpenObserve + OpenTelemetry | Logs, metrics, traces |

---

## Pricing/Billing Model

Raven has a **tiered SaaS model** with three subscription plans:

### Free Plan
- **Price**: $0 forever
- **Max Users**: 5
- **Max Workspaces**: 2
- **Max Knowledge Bases**: 3
- **Max Storage**: 500 MB
- **Concurrent Voice Sessions**: 1
- **Voice Minutes/Month**: 60
- **Features**: Full MIT source, unlimited documents, AI chat/voice/WhatsApp, BYOK

### Pro Plan
- **Price**: $29/month
- **Max Users**: 25
- **Max Workspaces**: 10
- **Max Knowledge Bases**: 50
- **Max Storage**: 10 GB
- **Concurrent Voice Sessions**: 5
- **Voice Minutes/Month**: 1,200
- **Additional Features**: Advanced rate limiting (per config: 600 req/min general, 120 req/min completion)

### Enterprise Plan
- **Price**: Custom (contact sales)
- **Max Users**: Unlimited (-1)
- **Max Workspaces**: Unlimited
- **Max Knowledge Bases**: Unlimited
- **Max Storage**: Unlimited
- **Concurrent Voice Sessions**: Unlimited
- **Voice Minutes/Month**: Unlimited
- **Additional Features**: SAML/SSO/SCIM, immutable audit logs, eBPF WAF, SOC2/GDPR compliance

**Payment Integration**: Hyperswitch payment orchestrator with Razorpay as the connector. Webhook-based idempotent event processing (payment_succeeded, payment_failed, subscription_cancelled).

---

## Target Audience

1. **Development Teams** — Prefer self-hosting, full control over LLM keys and data
2. **Enterprise Organizations** — Need multi-tenancy, RLS isolation, SSO/SCIM
3. **Edge/IoT Deployments** — 25MB ARM64 binary runs on constrained hardware
4. **Knowledge-Heavy Industries** — Support customer queries via chat/voice/WhatsApp without manual work
5. **Cost-Conscious Orgs** — MIT open-source means zero licensing overhead

---

## Existing Landing Page & Marketing Content

**Landing Page**: `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/landing/index.html` (46KB static HTML with Tailwind)
- Hero section emphasizing "open-source" + "MIT licensed" + "4 deployment options"
- Features section with 6 cards (chat, voice, WhatsApp, hybrid search, multi-tenant, BYOK)
- Pricing table with 4 editions: Self-Hosted ($0), Self-Hosted+Enterprise (custom), Managed Cloud, and Cloud Pro
- Navigation to GitHub, sign-in, and "Get Started Free"
- Stats bar: MIT licensed, 4 deployment options, unlimited knowledge sources

**Admin Dashboard**: `/frontend/src/pages/` (Vue.js SPA with 18 page directories)
- Billing pages (subscription management, usage tracking)
- Onboarding wizard (Keycloak realm auto-provisioning)
- Knowledge base management
- Voice session UI
- WhatsApp calling UI
- LLM provider configuration
- API keys management

---

## Current Development State

**All major phases complete** (as of April 2026):
- ✅ Phase 1: MVP (chatbot, ingestion, hybrid search, dashboard)
- ✅ Phase 2: Voice agent (LiveKit)
- ✅ Phase 3: WebRTC/WhatsApp integration
- 🔵 Phase 4 (In Planning): Knowledge graph (Neo4j, multi-hop queries)
- 🔵 Phase 5 (Future): Cloud managed offering, multi-region, SOC2

**Recent Focus** (latest PRs):
- Billing and subscription management UI
- Mobile-first responsive redesign (bottom tab bar, card views, touch targets)
- E2E tests (Playwright)
- Onboarding wizard with Keycloak realm auto-provisioning
- eBPF edge optimization (XDP pre-filtering, kernel observability)

---

## Deployment Options

1. **Docker Compose** (local development, small deployments)
2. **Kubernetes** (production, multi-region)
3. **EC2 + Ansible** (cloud VM with auto-TLS via Traefik)
4. **Cloudflare Pages** (frontend CDN)
5. **Edge** (Raspberry Pi + remote AI worker via gRPC)

---

## Frontend UI Capabilities

- **Admin Dashboard**: Organizations, workspaces, knowledge bases, billing, voice sessions, WhatsApp calls
- **Embeddable Chatbot Widget**: `<raven-chat>` web component with domain-scoped API keys
- **Mobile-First**: Bottom navigation, card layouts, responsive forms
- **Feature Flags**: PostHog integration for A/B testing
- **Quota Enforcement**: Automatic downgrade to free tier on failed payment (402 status code)

---

## Key Files for Marketing Reference

- `/README.md` — Product overview and architecture
- `/landing/index.html` — Current landing page (good reference for messaging)
- `/DEVELOPMENT.md` — Development guide (good for "Get Started" sections)
- `/docs/quickstart.md` — Quick setup instructions
- `/docs/project-status.md` — Roadmap and release history
- `/internal/model/billing.go` — Exact pricing tiers and limits
- `/internal/config/config.go` — Rate limiting by tier (60-600 req/min free-to-pro, unlimited enterprise)

---

## Messaging Pillars for Landing Page

1. **Open Source & Control** — MIT licensed, self-hostable, zero vendor lock-in
2. **BYOK Philosophy** — Bring your own LLM keys (Claude, OpenAI, Cohere)
3. **Multi-Channel** — One platform for chat, voice, and WhatsApp
4. **Data Sovereignty** — Full RLS isolation, PostgreSQL-backed, no SaaS escrow
5. **Enterprise Ready** — Keycloak/OIDC, SAML/SSO/SCIM, audit logs, eBPF WAF
6. **Simple Deployment** — Single Docker Compose file or 25MB ARM64 binary

This should give you everything needed to brief designers and copywriters for the marketing/landing page refresh!

1	# Visual Companion Guide
2	
3	Browser-based visual brainstorming companion for showing mockups, diagrams, and options.
4	
5	## When to Use
6	
7	Decide per-question, not per-session. The test: **would the user understand this better by seeing it than reading it?**
8	
9	**Use the browser** when the content itself is visual:
10	
11	- **UI mockups** — wireframes, layouts, navigation structures, component designs
12	- **Architecture diagrams** — system components, data flow, relationship maps
13	- **Side-by-side visual comparisons** — comparing two layouts, two color schemes, two design directions
14	- **Design polish** — when the question is about look and feel, spacing, visual hierarchy
15	- **Spatial relationships** — state machines, flowcharts, entity relationships rendered as diagrams
16	
17	**Use the terminal** when the content is text or tabular:
18	
19	- **Requirements and scope questions** — "what does X mean?", "which features are in scope?"
20	- **Conceptual A/B/C choices** — picking between approaches described in words
21	- **Tradeoff lists** — pros/cons, comparison tables
22	- **Technical decisions** — API design, data modeling, architectural approach selection
23	- **Clarifying questions** — anything where the answer is words, not a visual preference
24	
25	A question *about* a UI topic is not automatically a visual question. "What kind of wizard do you want?" is conceptual — use the terminal. "Which of these wizard layouts feels right?" is visual — use the browser.
26	
27	## How It Works
28	
29	The server watches a directory for HTML files and serves the newest one to the browser. You write HTML content, the user sees it in their browser and can click to select options. Selections are recorded to a `.events` file that you read on your next turn.
30	
31	**Content fragments vs full documents:** If your HTML file starts with `<!DOCTYPE` or `<html`, the server serves it as-is (just injects the helper script). Otherwise, the server automatically wraps your content in the frame template — adding the header, CSS theme, selection indicator, and all interactive infrastructure. **Write content fragments by default.** Only write full documents when you need complete control over the page.
32	
33	## Starting a Session
34	
35	```bash
36	# Start server with persistence (mockups saved to project)
37	scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
38	
39	# Returns: {"type":"server-started","port":52341,"url":"http://localhost:52341",
40	#           "screen_dir":"/path/to/project/.superpowers/brainstorm/12345-1706000000"}
41	```
42	
43	Save `screen_dir` from the response. Tell user to open the URL.
44	
45	**Finding connection info:** The server writes its startup JSON to `$SCREEN_DIR/.server-info`. If you launched the server in the background and didn't capture stdout, read that file to get the URL and port. When using `--project-dir`, check `<project>/.superpowers/brainstorm/` for the session directory.
46	
47	**Note:** Pass the project root as `--project-dir` so mockups persist in `.superpowers/brainstorm/` and survive server restarts. Without it, files go to `/tmp` and get cleaned up. Remind the user to add `.superpowers/` to `.gitignore` if it's not already there.
48	
49	**Launching the server by platform:**
50	
51	**Claude Code (macOS / Linux):**
52	```bash
53	# Default mode works — the script backgrounds the server itself
54	scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
55	```
56	
57	**Claude Code (Windows):**
58	```bash
59	# Windows auto-detects and uses foreground mode, which blocks the tool call.
60	# Use run_in_background: true on the Bash tool call so the server survives
61	# across conversation turns.
62	scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
63	```
64	When calling this via the Bash tool, set `run_in_background: true`. Then read `$SCREEN_DIR/.server-info` on the next turn to get the URL and port.
65	
66	**Codex:**
67	```bash
68	# Codex reaps background processes. The script auto-detects CODEX_CI and
69	# switches to foreground mode. Run it normally — no extra flags needed.
70	scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
71	```
72	
73	**Gemini CLI:**
74	```bash
75	# Use --foreground and set is_background: true on your shell tool call
76	# so the process survives across turns
77	scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project --foreground
78	```
79	
80	**Other environments:** The server must keep running in the background across conversation turns. If your environment reaps detached processes, use `--foreground` and launch the command with your platform's background execution mechanism.
81	
82	If the URL is unreachable from your browser (common in remote/containerized setups), bind a non-loopback host:
83	
84	```bash
85	scripts/start-server.sh \
86	  --project-dir /path/to/project \
87	  --host 0.0.0.0 \
88	  --url-host localhost
89	```
90	
91	Use `--url-host` to control what hostname is printed in the returned URL JSON.
92	
93	## The Loop
94	
95	1. **Check server is alive**, then **write HTML** to a new file in `screen_dir`:
96	   - Before each write, check that `$SCREEN_DIR/.server-info` exists. If it doesn't (or `.server-stopped` exists), the server has shut down — restart it with `start-server.sh` before continuing. The server auto-exits after 30 minutes of inactivity.
97	   - Use semantic filenames: `platform.html`, `visual-style.html`, `layout.html`
98	   - **Never reuse filenames** — each screen gets a fresh file
99	   - Use Write tool — **never use cat/heredoc** (dumps noise into terminal)
100	   - Server automatically serves the newest file
101	
102	2. **Tell user what to expect and end your turn:**
103	   - Remind them of the URL (every step, not just first)
104	   - Give a brief text summary of what's on screen (e.g., "Showing 3 layout options for the homepage")
105	   - Ask them to respond in the terminal: "Take a look and let me know what you think. Click to select an option if you'd like."
106	
107	3. **On your next turn** — after the user responds in the terminal:
108	   - Read `$SCREEN_DIR/.events` if it exists — this contains the user's browser interactions (clicks, selections) as JSON lines
109	   - Merge with the user's terminal text to get the full picture
110	   - The terminal message is the primary feedback; `.events` provides structured interaction data
111	
112	4. **Iterate or advance** — if feedback changes current screen, write a new file (e.g., `layout-v2.html`). Only move to the next question when the current step is validated.
113	
114	5. **Unload when returning to terminal** — when the next step doesn't need the browser (e.g., a clarifying question, a tradeoff discussion), push a waiting screen to clear the stale content:
115	
116	   ```html
117	   <!-- filename: waiting.html (or waiting-2.html, etc.) -->
118	   <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;min-height:60vh">
119	     <p class="subtitle">Continuing in terminal...</p>
120	   </div>
121	   ```
122	
123	   This prevents the user from staring at a resolved choice while the conversation has moved on. When the next visual question comes up, push a new content file as usual.
124	
125	6. Repeat until done.
126	
127	## Writing Content Fragments
128	
129	Write just the content that goes inside the page. The server wraps it in the frame template automatically (header, theme CSS, selection indicator, and all interactive infrastructure).
130	
131	**Minimal example:**
132	
133	```html
134	<h2>Which layout works better?</h2>
135	<p class="subtitle">Consider readability and visual hierarchy</p>
136	
137	<div class="options">
138	  <div class="option" data-choice="a" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
139	    <div class="letter">A</div>
140	    <div class="content">
141	      <h3>Single Column</h3>
142	      <p>Clean, focused reading experience</p>
143	    </div>
144	  </div>
145	  <div class="option" data-choice="b" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
146	    <div class="letter">B</div>
147	    <div class="content">
148	      <h3>Two Column</h3>
149	      <p>Sidebar navigation with main content</p>
150	    </div>
151	  </div>
152	</div>
153	```
154	
155	That's it. No `<html>`, no CSS, no `<script>` tags needed. The server provides all of that.
156	
157	## CSS Classes Available
158	
159	The frame template provides these CSS classes for your content:
160	
161	### Options (A/B/C choices)
162	
163	```html
164	<div class="options">
165	  <div class="option" data-choice="a" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
166	    <div class="letter">A</div>
167	    <div class="content">
168	      <h3>Title</h3>
169	      <p>Description</p>
170	    </div>
171	  </div>
172	</div>
173	```
174	
175	**Multi-select:** Add `data-multiselect` to the container to let users select multiple options. Each click toggles the item. The indicator bar shows the count.
176	
177	```html
178	<div class="options" data-multiselect>
179	  <!-- same option markup — users can select/deselect multiple -->
180	</div>
181	```
182	
183	### Cards (visual designs)
184	
185	```html
186	<div class="cards">
187	  <div class="card" data-choice="design1" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
188	    <div class="card-image"><!-- mockup content --></div>
189	    <div class="card-body">
190	      <h3>Name</h3>
191	      <p>Description</p>
192	    </div>
193	  </div>
194	</div>
195	```
196	
197	### Mockup container
198	
199	```html
200	<div class="mockup">
201	  <div class="mockup-header">Preview: Dashboard Layout</div>
202	  <div class="mockup-body"><!-- your mockup HTML --></div>
203	</div>
204	```
205	
206	### Split view (side-by-side)
207	
208	```html
209	<div class="split">
210	  <div class="mockup"><!-- left --></div>
211	  <div class="mockup"><!-- right --></div>
212	</div>
213	```
214	
215	### Pros/Cons
216	
217	```html
218	<div class="pros-cons">
219	  <div class="pros"><h4>Pros</h4><ul><li>Benefit</li></ul></div>
220	  <div class="cons"><h4>Cons</h4><ul><li>Drawback</li></ul></div>
221	</div>
222	```
223	
224	### Mock elements (wireframe building blocks)
225	
226	```html
227	<div class="mock-nav">Logo | Home | About | Contact</div>
228	<div style="display: flex;">
229	  <div class="mock-sidebar">Navigation</div>
230	  <div class="mock-content">Main content area</div>
231	</div>
232	<button class="mock-button">Action Button</button>
233	<input class="mock-input" placeholder="Input field">
234	<div class="placeholder">Placeholder area</div>
235	```
236	
237	### Typography and sections
238	
239	- `h2` — page title
240	- `h3` — section heading
241	- `.subtitle` — secondary text below title
242	- `.section` — content block with bottom margin
243	- `.label` — small uppercase label text
244	
245	## Browser Events Format
246	
247	When the user clicks options in the browser, their interactions are recorded to `$SCREEN_DIR/.events` (one JSON object per line). The file is cleared automatically when you push a new screen.
248	
249	```jsonl
250	{"type":"click","choice":"a","text":"Option A - Simple Layout","timestamp":1706000101}
251	{"type":"click","choice":"c","text":"Option C - Complex Grid","timestamp":1706000108}
252	{"type":"click","choice":"b","text":"Option B - Hybrid","timestamp":1706000115}
253	```
254	
255	The full event stream shows the user's exploration path — they may click multiple options before settling. The last `choice` event is typically the final selection, but the pattern of clicks can reveal hesitation or preferences worth asking about.
256	
257	If `.events` doesn't exist, the user didn't interact with the browser — use only their terminal text.
258	
259	## Design Tips
260	
261	- **Scale fidelity to the question** — wireframes for layout, polish for polish questions
262	- **Explain the question on each page** — "Which layout feels more professional?" not just "Pick one"
263	- **Iterate before advancing** — if feedback changes current screen, write a new version
264	- **2-4 options max** per screen
265	- **Use real content when it matters** — for a photography portfolio, use actual images (Unsplash). Placeholder content obscures design issues.
266	- **Keep mockups simple** — focus on layout and structure, not pixel-perfect design
267	
268	## File Naming
269	
270	- Use semantic names: `platform.html`, `visual-style.html`, `layout.html`
271	- Never reuse filenames — each screen must be a new file
272	- For iterations: append version suffix like `layout-v2.html`, `layout-v3.html`
273	- Server serves newest file by modification time
274	
275	## Cleaning Up
276	
277	```bash
278	scripts/stop-server.sh $SCREEN_DIR
279	```
280	
281	If the session used `--project-dir`, mockup files persist in `.superpowers/brainstorm/` for later reference. Only `/tmp` sessions get deleted on stop.
282	
283	## Reference
284	
285	- Frame template (CSS reference): `scripts/frame-template.html`
286	- Helper script (client-side): `scripts/helper.js`
287	
```

Next question: Looking at your existing pricing model (Free $0 / Pro $29/mo / Enterprise custom), I noticed the payment stack is Hyperswitch + Razorpay. For the landing page pricing section, which approach do you want?

- **A) Show all three tiers with prices** — Free ($0), Pro ($29/mo), Enterprise (Contact Sales) — like Infisical does
- **B) Lead with "Self-host free, forever" and only show Cloud pricing** — separate the open-source self-hosted story from the managed cloud pricing, emphasizing the OSS angle harder
- **C) Usage-based transparency** — show the tier limits prominently (5 users/500MB free, 25 users/10GB pro) so people self-select, similar to Supabase's approach

Which feels right for where Raven is today?

> DEVELOPER

go for the recommended one

> AGENT

My recommendation is **A — show all three tiers with prices**, but borrow the best of C by making the limits prominent in each tier card. Here's why:

- Raven is early stage — you want **maximum clarity** so visitors instantly understand what they get
- The free tier is genuinely generous (5 users, 500MB, voice minutes, full self-host) — showing it builds trust
- Limit-based self-selection (like Supabase) removes friction — people see exactly when they'd need to upgrade
- "Contact Sales" for Enterprise creates conversation opportunities with high-value prospects
- Option B (splitting self-host vs cloud) adds complexity you don't need yet

Next question: **Who is the primary visitor you're optimizing this page for?**

- **A) Developer/technical lead** evaluating Raven for their team — wants to see architecture, GitHub link, Docker quickstart, API docs
- **B) Business decision maker** (CTO/VP Eng) — wants to see ROI, compliance badges, enterprise features, case studies
- **C) Both equally** — developer-first copy with enterprise trust signals layered in (the Infisical approach)

I'd recommend **A + C combined**: embed the actual `<raven-chat>` widget as a live demo (it's your strongest differentiator — visitors can *talk* to an AI knowledge base immediately), and use tabbed product screenshots for the feature deep-dive sections. The live widget is a conversion weapon most competitors can't match.

- **B) "Open source AI knowledge platform"** — category-first, no competitor callout, let the product speak for itself (how Supabase positions vs Firebase without always naming them on the hero)

I'd go with **B — category-first positioning**. Reasoning:

- Raven doesn't have one obvious competitor to position against — it's a unique combo of chat + voice + WhatsApp over a knowledge base
- Naming a competitor (Option A) works when you're a drop-in replacement (Supabase vs Firebase), but Raven is carving a new category
- The deployment freedom story (C) is powerful but works better as a trust signal *below* the hero, not the lead message
- "Open-source AI knowledge platform" lets you own the category rather than riding someone else's brand

The hero headline should establish *what* Raven is, then the sections below layer in the self-host/cloud/edge story as proof of commitment to openness.

- **C) Bold and opinionated** — "Your team's knowledge is trapped in docs nobody reads. Fix that." — provocative, problem-first storytelling

Now let me propose approaches. Here are **3 structural approaches** for the landing page:

---

### Approach 1: "The Infisical Clone" (Faithful Adaptation)
Mirror Infisical's exact section structure adapted for Raven:
- Nav → Hero → Logo bar (replaced with trust badges) → Tabbed product features → Vanity metric → Governance/Enterprise cards → Integrations grid → Compliance section → Bottom CTA → Footer

**Pros**: Proven SaaS conversion pattern, fast to build, familiar to visitors
**Cons**: Could feel generic, doesn't leverage Raven's unique live demo advantage

### Approach 2: "Demo-Led Landing" (Recommended)
Lead with the live `<raven-chat>` widget as the centerpiece, restructured around *experiencing* the product:
- Nav → Hero with embedded live chatbot → Trust badges → Problem statement → Tabbed features (Chat/Voice/WhatsApp) with screenshots → Pricing → Deploy options → Bottom CTA → Footer

**Pros**: Unique differentiator front-and-center, visitors engage immediately, higher conversion potential
**Cons**: Requires a working demo knowledge base, slightly more complex to maintain

### Approach 3: "Story-Driven"
Problem-first narrative flow that walks visitors through the pain → solution → proof:
- Nav → Problem hero ("Your team's knowledge is scattered") → Solution reveal → Feature sections → Live demo section → Pricing → Deploy → Footer

**Pros**: Emotionally compelling, great for B2B audiences
**Cons**: Longer page, demo is buried lower, slower to hook technical visitors

---

**My recommendation: Approach 2** — the live chatbot widget is something Infisical *can't* do. It turns your landing page from "read about our product" into "try our product right now." Combine it with Infisical's proven section patterns for everything else.

Which approach appeals to you?

Now section flow was approved. Let me present the **problem statement section** — this is the emotional hook right below the hero.

**Section 3: Problem Statement**

> **"Your team's knowledge is scattered. Your people are stuck."**

Three pain-point cards:

| Card | Headline | Copy |
|------|----------|------|
| 1 | **Docs nobody reads** | Knowledge lives in PDFs, wikis, and Confluence pages that go stale. Your team asks the same questions on Slack instead. |
| 2 | **Search that doesn't work** | Keyword search returns 50 results. None of them answer the actual question. Your team gives up and pings someone senior. |
| 3 | **No way to just ask** | Your team wants to ask a question and get an answer — with a source. Not browse. Not search. Just ask. |

Then a transition line:
> **"Raven fixes this. Ingest your docs. Ask in natural language. Get cited answers — via chat, voice, or WhatsApp."**

**Section 5: How It Works — 3-step flow**

| Step | Headline | Copy |
|------|----------|------|
| 1 | **Ingest** | Upload PDFs, DOCX, Markdown, HTML, or paste URLs. Raven chunks, embeds, and indexes everything automatically. |
| 2 | **Search** | Hybrid retrieval — pgvector cosine similarity + BM25 full-text ranking fused with Reciprocal Rank Fusion. No keyword guessing. |
| 3 | **Answer** | Your team asks in natural language. Raven responds with accurate, cited answers — via chat, voice, or WhatsApp. |

Minimal visual — three numbered cards in a horizontal row with an arrow/line connecting them. Technical details surface on hover or in small muted text below each card.

**Section 6: Deploy Your Way — 4 options**

| Option | Icon | Copy |
|--------|------|------|
| Docker Compose | 🐳 | One command. Full stack. Perfect for getting started and small teams. |
| Kubernetes | ☸️ | Production-grade, multi-region, auto-scaling. Helm charts included. |
| EC2 + Ansible | 🖥️ | Cloud VM with auto-TLS via Traefik. Playbooks ready to go. |
| Edge / Raspberry Pi | 📡 | 25MB ARM64 binary. Run the API on a Pi, connect a remote AI worker via gRPC. |

**Section 7: Enterprise Ready — feature cards**

| Card | Copy |
|------|------|
| **Multi-Tenant Isolation** | Full Row-Level Security per tenant in PostgreSQL. Your data never crosses boundaries. |
| **SSO / SCIM** | Keycloak-powered OIDC, SAML, and SCIM provisioning out of the box. |
| **Audit Logs** | Immutable, timestamped audit trail for every action. Compliance-ready. |
| **BYOK** | Bring your own LLM keys — Anthropic, OpenAI, Cohere, or self-hosted models. Zero vendor lock-in. |
| **eBPF WAF** | Kernel-level XDP pre-filtering and observability at the edge. |
| **GDPR / SOC2** | Built for compliance. Self-host for full data sovereignty. |

(anonymous users, read-only)\n\n### Performance Targets\n- First Contentful Paint < 1.5s\n- Total page weight < 500KB (excluding chat widget)\n- Lighthouse score > 90 across all categories\n\n## Open Questions\n\n1. **Raven logo**: Does a finalized SVG logo exist, or do we need to create one?\n2. **Demo instance**: Is there a running demo API we can point the chat widget to, or do we need to set one up?\n3. **Product screenshots**: Can we capture these from the existing admin dashboard, or do we need to mock them?\n4. **Domain setup**: Is raven.ravencloak.org DNS already configured?\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- User authentication / sign-up flow (handled by app.ravencloak.org)\n- Blog / changelog pages (future addition)\n- Documentation site (separate docs.ravencloak.org)\n- A/B testing / analytics integration (PostHog — future addition)\n"
}

I now have a thorough picture of the codebase state versus what the spec promises. Here is my review.

---

## Design Spec Review: Raven Landing Page

**File**: `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-04-13-raven-landing-page-design.md`

---

### What the spec does well

- The section structure is logical and follows a proven SaaS landing page funnel: problem, solution, features, social proof, pricing, CTA.
- The design token table is unusually thorough for a spec -- it provides both hex values and Tailwind class names for both themes, which will make implementation fast and unambiguous.
- The pricing table matches the backend model exactly. I verified the tiers, limits, and prices against `internal/model/billing.go` (`DefaultPlans()`), and the numbers are a 1:1 match: Free (5 users, 2 workspaces, 3 KBs, 500 MB, 1 concurrent voice, 60 min/mo), Pro ($29/mo, 25 users, etc.), Enterprise ($99/mo backend but "Contact Sales" on the landing page -- see issue below).
- The `<raven-chat>` web component is real and production-ready. It exists at `frontend/src/components/chat-widget/`, supports all the attributes the spec implies (api-key, api-url, theme-color, voice-enabled, etc.), and has a clean custom-element registration path.

---

### MUST-FIX Issues

**1. Enterprise pricing discrepancy between spec and backend**

The spec says Enterprise is "Contact Sales" with no listed price. However, `internal/model/billing.go` defines the enterprise plan at `PriceMonthly: 9900` ($99.00/mo). The spec needs to decide: is Enterprise truly custom/contact-sales, or is it a self-serve $99/mo tier? If "Contact Sales" is the intended landing page presentation, that is fine for marketing, but the spec should explicitly note the discrepancy so implementers do not accidentally show "$99/mo" somewhere. Add a one-line note like "Enterprise is priced at $99/mo in the backend but presented as Contact Sales on the landing page."

**2. Color scheme completely contradicts the existing landing page**

The spec mandates a black/white + amber accent palette. The existing `landing/index.html` (already shipped) uses an indigo/purple (`raven-500: #6366f1`) color scheme with a purple gradient hero, purple buttons, and no amber anywhere. The chat widget's default theme color is also `#6366f1` (indigo), not amber. The spec needs to acknowledge this is a full redesign, not an iteration. It should state whether the existing `landing/index.html` is being replaced wholesale or whether this is a new page at a different path. Without this clarity, an implementer might try to incrementally modify the existing page and create an incoherent hybrid.

**3. Chat widget API is still mock-only -- demo will not work as described**

The spec says the hero will contain a "live" `<raven-chat>` widget "connected to a demo knowledge base" with "pre-populated sample Q&A showing cited answer." I verified `frontend/src/components/chat-widget/chat-api.ts` and it is entirely a mock implementation with `TODO: Replace mock implementation with real SSE fetch` comments. It returns canned random responses, not real RAG answers, and does not produce citations. The spec should either (a) add a prerequisite task to implement the real SSE endpoint before the landing page, or (b) revise the demo description to say "mock-mode demo" and drop the "cited answer" claim, or (c) list the real API endpoint as a dependency in the Open Questions section.

**4. "Helm charts included" claim has no backing artifact**

Section 6 (Deploy Your Way) states Kubernetes deployment with "Helm charts included." There is no `deploy/kubernetes/` directory, no `helm/` directory, and no Helm chart files anywhere in the repository. Either remove "Helm charts included" from the spec copy, or add a task to create them before the landing page ships. Advertising non-existent artifacts on a marketing page is a credibility risk.

**5. "Audit Logs" and "SCIM" enterprise features are not implemented**

Section 7 (Enterprise Ready) claims "Immutable, timestamped audit trail for every action" and "SCIM provisioning out of the box." Searching the codebase, there is no audit log implementation (only references in test stubs and specs) and no SCIM implementation at all. These features exist only in design docs and roadmap. The spec should either label these as "Coming Soon" on the landing page or remove them. Presenting unimplemented features as current capabilities is misleading.

**6. No SEO/meta tags or Open Graph specification**

The existing `landing/index.html` already has `<meta>` tags (og:title, og:description, og:url, twitter:card). The spec defines no meta tags, page title, or OG image. Since this is a full redesign hosted at a different domain (`raven.ravencloak.org` vs the current page's `ravencloak.org`), the spec must include the SEO/meta section to avoid losing existing SEO or shipping without social sharing cards. At minimum: page title, meta description, og:image, og:url, canonical URL, and favicon.

**7. Typography spec lacks concrete values**

The Typography section uses vague descriptors ("Bold/extrabold, larger sizes") without specifying font family, font sizes, line heights, or Tailwind utility classes. For a static HTML + Tailwind implementation, the implementer needs to know: Which font? (The existing page uses Inter.) What are the heading sizes at each breakpoint? Without this, two implementers will produce different-looking pages. At minimum, specify the font family and the size scale (e.g., `text-4xl sm:text-6xl lg:text-7xl` for hero heading).

---

### NICE-TO-HAVE Improvements

**8. Missing font specification**

The existing landing page loads Inter via Google Fonts. The spec should either explicitly carry this forward or specify an alternative. This is downstream of issue 7 but worth a separate callout since font loading has performance implications (the spec targets FCP < 1.5s).

**9. No animation/interaction specification**

The spec describes static content but says nothing about scroll animations, section transitions, hover states beyond color changes, or the tab-switching mechanism for the Features section. The existing page has CSS keyframe animations (`float`, `feature-card:hover`). Should the new page have entrance animations? Smooth scroll? The tabbed features section (Section 4) needs explicit interaction design: how do tabs switch, is there a transition between screenshots, what happens on mobile (do tabs become a vertical stack or a swipeable carousel)?

**10. "Try Live Demo" CTA scroll anchor not defined**

The hero has a `Try Live Demo` ghost button. The spec does not specify what this scrolls to. Presumably it scrolls to the embedded chat widget, but the widget is described as being inside the hero section itself, not below the fold. Clarify whether clicking this button (a) opens the chat widget, (b) smooth-scrolls to a widget below the hero, or (c) navigates to a separate demo page.

**11. GitHub stars badge not specified**

The nav mentions "GitHub (with stars badge)" but does not specify how to fetch the star count (static? GitHub API? shields.io badge?). For a static HTML page, a dynamic star count requires either a client-side fetch to the GitHub API (with rate limiting concerns for anonymous users) or a build-time injection. Specify the approach.

**12. Tailwind CSS distribution strategy unclear**

The spec says "Tailwind CSS (paid license -- Tailwind Plus/UI components)" but the existing landing page uses the CDN play script (`<script src="https://cdn.tailwindcss.com">`). For production at the stated performance targets (<500KB page weight, Lighthouse >90), the CDN play script is inappropriate -- it ships the entire Tailwind runtime (~300KB). The spec should state whether to use Tailwind CLI with purging or Vite + PostCSS. This directly impacts whether the 500KB budget and FCP target are achievable.

**13. Domain inconsistency in existing assets**

The spec targets `raven.ravencloak.org`. The existing `landing/index.html` uses `og:url` of `https://ravencloak.org`. The chat widget's "Powered by" link points to `https://ravenapp.dev`. CTA links point to `app.ravencloak.org`. The spec should provide a definitive domain map (marketing site, app, API, docs) so all links are consistent.

**14. Responsive breakpoint behavior not detailed for key sections**

The spec says "Mobile-first, breakpoints at sm, md, lg, xl" but only gives vague "stacks on mobile" notes for each section. The tabbed features section (Section 4) -- which alternates screenshot left/right -- needs explicit mobile treatment. Do the tabs become an accordion? A vertical list? Does the alternating layout collapse to a consistent order?

**15. No accessibility specification beyond basic semantics**

For a Lighthouse score >90 across all categories (which includes accessibility), the spec should call out: color contrast ratios for the amber accent on black/white backgrounds, focus ring styling, reduced-motion preferences, and aria-label patterns. The amber-400 (#fbbf24) on black passes WCAG AA (contrast ratio ~11.6:1), but amber-400 on the secondary dark background (#0a0a0a) should be verified. More critically, the muted text (#737373) on the dark background (#000000) has a contrast ratio of ~4.6:1 -- it passes AA for normal text but fails AAA.

**16. Open Questions should block implementation**

The Open Questions section lists 4 items (logo SVG, demo API, screenshots, DNS) but marks them as open questions rather than blocking prerequisites. At least items 2 (demo instance) and 4 (DNS) are hard blockers -- you cannot ship a landing page at a domain that is not configured, and the demo-led hero is the centerpiece of the design. These should be upgraded to prerequisites with owners and deadlines.

---

### Summary

The spec is well-structured and the section-by-section content design is strong. The pricing data is verified accurate against the backend. However, there are 7 must-fix issues that would cause implementation confusion or result in a page that makes claims the codebase cannot back up (mock demo presented as live, Helm charts that do not exist, unimplemented enterprise features). The color scheme change from the existing page also needs explicit acknowledgment. Resolving these before writing an implementation plan will prevent rework.

Ingest your docs, ask in natural language, get cited answers via chat, voice, or WhatsApp. Self-host or use our cloud.\">\n<meta property=\"og:title\" content=\"Raven — Open Source AI Knowledge Platform\">\n<meta property=\"og:description\" content=\"Ingest your docs. Ask in natural language. Get cited answers via chat, voice, or WhatsApp.\">\n<meta property=\"og:url\" content=\"https://raven.ravencloak.org\">\n<meta property=\"og:image\" content=\"https://raven.ravencloak.org/og-image.png\">\n<meta property=\"og:type\" content=\"website\">\n<meta name=\"twitter:card\" content=\"summary_large_image\">\n<link rel=\"canonical\" href=\"https://raven.ravencloak.org\">\n```\n\nOG image: 1200x630px, Raven logo + tagline on dark background. Created as a static asset.\n\n### Domain Map\n\n| Purpose | Domain |\n|---------|--------|\n| Marketing/landing page | `raven.ravencloak.org` |\n| Application (dashboard) | `app.ravencloak.org` |\n| API server | `api.ravencloak.org` |\n| Auth (Keycloak) | `auth.ravencloak.org` |\n| Documentation | `docs.ravencloak.org` (future) |\n\n### Accessibility\n\n- Amber accent (`#fbbf24`) on black background: contrast ratio ~11.6:1 (passes WCAG AA and AAA)\n- Muted text (`#737373`) on black: contrast ratio ~4.6:1 (passes AA for normal text)\n- All interactive elements: visible focus ring (`ring-2 ring-amber-400 ring-offset-2 ring-offset-black`)\n- `prefers-reduced-motion`: disable scroll animations\n- Semantic HTML: `<nav>`, `<main>`, `<section>`, `<footer>` with `aria-label` on each section\n\n### Interactions & Animations\n\n- **Scroll animations**: Subtle fade-in-up on section entry (via `IntersectionObserver`), respects `prefers-reduced-motion`\n- **Tab switching** (Section 4): Click to switch, amber underline slides to active tab, screenshot crossfades. On mobile: tabs become a vertical accordion\n- **Hover states**: Cards lift with `hover:-translate-y-1 hover:border-amber-400/50` transition\n- **Theme toggle**: Smooth color transition via `transition-colors duration-300` on `<html>`\n- **GitHub stars**: Static count at build time (hardcoded, updated periodically) — avoids GitHub API rate limits"
}

Now I have the complete catalog. Here is the report.

---

## Existing State

The project at `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/landing/index.html` already has a hand-built landing page using Tailwind CSS via CDN (not Tailwind Plus components). There are zero references to Tailwind UI/Plus imports, `@tailwindcss/ui`, or any copied template code anywhere in the repo.

## Tailwind Plus Marketing Component Mapping

The Tailwind Plus "Marketing" package provides these categories. Here is the best match for each landing page section:

| # | Landing Section | Tailwind Plus Category | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Nav** | **Marketing > Elements > Headers** (11 components) | Drop-in responsive navbar with mobile menu. Direct match. |
| 2 | **Hero** | **Marketing > Page Sections > Hero Sections** (12 components) | Direct match. Pick one with centered text + dual CTAs to match current layout. |
| 3 | **Problem statement** | **Marketing > Page Sections > Stats** (8 components) | Stats sections work well for pain-point numbers. Alternatively, **Feature Sections** (15 components) with icon grid for problem framing. |
| 4 | **Tabbed features** | **Marketing > Page Sections > Feature Sections** (15 components) | Some include tab-style toggling. **Bento Grids** (3 components) also work if you want a visual grid. **Custom work needed** for true interactive tabs -- Feature Sections offer static alternating layouts, not full tab UIs out of the box. Pair with **Application UI > Navigation > Tabs** (9 components) for the tab control itself. |
| 5 | **How it works** | **Marketing > Page Sections > Content Sections** (7 components) | No dedicated timeline/steps component exists. **Custom work required** to build a numbered steps layout. Content Sections provide the closest structural base. |
| 6 | **Deploy options** | **Marketing > Page Sections > Bento Grids** (3 components) or **Feature Sections** | Bento grids are ideal for a 2-3 card deploy options layout (Cloud / Self-host / Edge). Direct match. |
| 7 | **Enterprise ready** | **Marketing > Page Sections > Feature Sections** (15 components) | Feature grid with icons (SSO, RBAC, audit logs, etc.). Direct match. **Logo Clouds** (6 components) could supplement with trust logos. |
| 8 | **Pricing** | **Marketing > Page Sections > Pricing Sections** (12 components) | Direct match. Multiple tier layouts available. |
| 9 | **Bottom CTA** | **Marketing > Page Sections > CTA Sections** (11 components) | Direct match. Simple centered or split CTA banners. |
| 10 | **Footer** | **Marketing > Page Sections > Footers** (16 components listed in sidebar) | Direct match. Multi-column footers with social links. |

## Sections Requiring Custom Work

- **Section 4 (Tabbed features)**: Tailwind Plus Feature Sections are static layouts. Interactive tab switching needs custom JS or combining with the Application UI Tabs component.
- **Section 5 (How it works / Steps)**: No dedicated timeline or numbered-steps component exists in Tailwind Plus. You will need to build this custom, using Content Sections as a structural starting point.

All other 8 sections have direct, ready-to-use Tailwind Plus component matches. The relevant SaaS marketing templates to reference for overall page structure are **Radiant** and **Oatmeal** (both labeled "SaaS marketing template/kit").

1	# Raven Landing Page — Design Spec
2	
3	**Date**: 2026-04-13
4	**Domain**: raven.ravencloak.org
5	**Status**: Approved
6	
7	---
8	
9	## Overview
10	
11	A full-platform marketing landing page for Raven — an open-source, self-hostable, multi-tenant AI knowledge platform with chat, voice, and WhatsApp capabilities. The page follows a demo-led approach with an embedded live `<raven-chat>` widget in the hero section.
12	
13	> **Note**: This is a **full redesign** replacing the existing `landing/index.html` (which uses an indigo/purple color scheme). The new page uses a black/white + amber palette and will be deployed at `raven.ravencloak.org`.
14	
15	## Target Audience
16	
17	- **Primary**: Developers and technical leads evaluating Raven for their team
18	- **Secondary**: Business decision makers (CTO/VP Eng) looking for enterprise features and compliance
19	- **Approach**: Developer-first copy with enterprise trust signals layered in
20	
21	## Design System
22	
23	### Color Palette (Tailwind)
24	
25	| Token | Dark Theme | Light Theme | Usage |
26	|-------|-----------|-------------|-------|
27	| Background primary | `#000000` / `black` | `#ffffff` / `white` | Page background |
28	| Background secondary | `#0a0a0a` / `neutral-950` | `#fafafa` / `neutral-50` | Card/section backgrounds |
29	| Background tertiary | `#1a1a1a` / `neutral-900` | `#f5f5f5` / `neutral-100` | Inset panels, code blocks |
30	| Border | `#262626` / `neutral-800` | `#e5e5e5` / `neutral-200` | Card borders, dividers |
31	| Border hover | `#404040` / `neutral-700` | `#d4d4d4` / `neutral-300` | Interactive borders |
32	| Text primary | `#ffffff` / `white` | `#000000` / `black` | Headlines, body |
33	| Text secondary | `#a3a3a3` / `neutral-400` | `#525252` / `neutral-600` | Descriptions, subtext |
34	| Text muted | `#737373` / `neutral-500` | `#737373` / `neutral-500` | Captions, labels |
35	| Accent | `#fbbf24` / `amber-400` | `#f59e0b` / `amber-500` | CTAs, active indicators, highlights |
36	| Accent hover | `#f59e0b` / `amber-500` | `#d97706` / `amber-600` | Button hover states |
37	
38	### Theme Behavior
39	
40	- Dark/light toggle in nav (sun/moon icon)
41	- Default: respects `prefers-color-scheme` system preference
42	- Implemented via Tailwind `dark:` class variant on `<html>` element
43	- Raven logo: black bird on light theme, white bird on dark theme
44	
45	### Typography
46	
47	- **Font family**: Inter (loaded via Google Fonts, same as existing landing page)
48	- **Hero headline**: `text-5xl sm:text-6xl lg:text-7xl font-extrabold tracking-tight`
49	- **Section headlines**: `text-3xl sm:text-4xl font-bold`
50	- **Card headlines**: `text-xl font-semibold`
51	- **Body copy**: `text-base sm:text-lg` (16-18px), `leading-relaxed`
52	- **Muted/technical text**: `text-sm text-neutral-500`
53	- **Badge text**: `text-xs uppercase tracking-widest font-medium`
54	
55	### Content Tone
56	
57	- **Section headers**: Bold and opinionated — *"Your team's knowledge is trapped. Free it."*
58	- **Body copy**: Clear and accessible — *"Ingest any document, ask in natural language, get cited answers."*
59	- **Feature deep-dives**: Technical specifics — *"Hybrid search combining pgvector cosine similarity with BM25 full-text ranking"*
60	- **GitHub/README**: Engineering-precise tone (separate from landing page)
61	
62	## Page Structure
63	
64	### Section 1: Navigation Bar
65	
66	**Layout**: Sticky top nav, full-width, blurred background
67	
68	**Elements**:
69	- Left: Raven logo (themed — black on light, white on dark)
70	- Center: Features, Pricing, Docs, GitHub (with stars badge)
71	- Right: Theme toggle (sun/moon), Sign In (ghost button), Get Started Free (amber accent button)
72	
73	**Mobile**: Hamburger menu, Get Started Free always visible
74	
75	### Section 2: Hero (Centered, Demo-Led)
76	
77	**Layout**: Centered text block → trust badges → live chat widget
78	
79	**Content**:
80	- Badge: `Open Source · MIT Licensed` (small uppercase, amber text)
81	- Headline: **"The AI Brain for Your Entire Team"**
82	- Subhead: "Ingest your docs. Ask in natural language. Get cited answers via chat, voice, or WhatsApp. Self-host or use our cloud."
83	- CTAs: `Get Started Free` (amber filled) + `Try Live Demo ↓` (ghost/outline)
84	- Trust bar: 🔓 MIT Licensed · 🐳 4 Deploy Options · 🔑 Bring Your Own LLM Keys · 🛡️ Full Data Sovereignty
85	
86	**Live Demo Widget**:
87	- Embedded `<raven-chat>` web component
88	- **Phase 1 (launch)**: Runs in mock mode with curated hardcoded Q&A pairs showcasing Raven's capabilities (the chat widget currently uses mock responses — real SSE endpoint is not yet implemented)
89	- **Phase 2 (post-launch)**: Connect to a live demo knowledge base once the real SSE chat API is implemented
90	- Header: "💬 Try Raven — Ask anything" + "Interactive Demo" badge
91	- Pre-populated with a sample Q&A showing the answer format (with citation styling)
92	- Input field with Send button (amber accent)
93	- "Try Live Demo ↓" CTA in hero smooth-scrolls to this widget
94	
95	### Section 3: Problem Statement
96	
97	**Headline**: "Your team's knowledge is scattered. Your people are stuck."
98	
99	**Layout**: 3 cards in a row (stacks on mobile)
100	
101	| Card | Headline | Copy |
102	|------|----------|------|
103	| 1 | Docs nobody reads | Knowledge lives in PDFs, wikis, and Confluence pages that go stale. Your team asks the same questions on Slack instead. |
104	| 2 | Search that doesn't work | Keyword search returns 50 results. None of them answer the actual question. Your team gives up and pings someone senior. |
105	| 3 | No way to just ask | Your team wants to ask a question and get an answer — with a source. Not browse. Not search. Just ask. |
106	
107	**Transition line**: "Raven fixes this. Ingest your docs. Ask in natural language. Get cited answers — via chat, voice, or WhatsApp."
108	
109	### Section 4: Tabbed Features
110	
111	**Layout**: 3 tabs with active indicator (amber underline). Each tab shows screenshot on one side + description on the other. Alternating layout (screenshot left/right) for visual rhythm.
112	
113	**Tab 1: AI Chat**
114	- Headline: "Ask your docs anything"
115	- Copy: Semantic search powered by hybrid vector + BM25 ranking. Every answer comes with source citations. Embed the chatbot widget on your site with a single `<raven-chat>` tag.
116	- Visual: Screenshot of chat interface with cited answer
117	
118	**Tab 2: Voice Agent**
119	- Headline: "Talk to your knowledge base"
120	- Copy: Real-time voice calls via WebRTC. Hands-free access to your entire knowledge base — powered by LiveKit with STT, LLM, and TTS pipeline. Perfect for field teams and mobile workers.
121	- Visual: Screenshot of voice call UI
122	
123	**Tab 3: WhatsApp**
124	- Headline: "Meet your team where they already are"
125	- Copy: Connect via WhatsApp Business API. Zero app installs, zero training. Your team asks questions in the chat they already use every day.
126	- Visual: Screenshot of WhatsApp conversation with Raven
127	
128	### Section 5: How It Works
129	
130	**Layout**: 3 numbered cards in horizontal row, connected by a line/arrow
131	
132	| Step | Headline | Copy |
133	|------|----------|------|
134	| 1 | **Ingest** | Upload PDFs, DOCX, Markdown, HTML, or paste URLs. Raven chunks, embeds, and indexes everything automatically. |
135	| 2 | **Search** | Hybrid retrieval — pgvector cosine similarity + BM25 full-text ranking fused with Reciprocal Rank Fusion. No keyword guessing. |
136	| 3 | **Answer** | Your team asks in natural language. Raven responds with accurate, cited answers — via chat, voice, or WhatsApp. |
137	
138	Technical details in muted text below each card for developer audience.
139	
140	### Section 6: Deploy Your Way
141	
142	**Headline**: "Your infrastructure. Your rules."
143	
144	**Layout**: 4 cards in a grid (2x2 on desktop, stacks on mobile)
145	
146	| Option | Icon | Copy |
147	|--------|------|------|
148	| Docker Compose | 🐳 | One command. Full stack. Perfect for getting started and small teams. |
149	| Kubernetes | ☸️ | Production-grade, multi-region, auto-scaling. |
150	| EC2 + Ansible | 🖥️ | Cloud VM with auto-TLS via Traefik. Playbooks ready to go. |
151	| Edge / Raspberry Pi | 📡 | 25MB ARM64 binary. Run the API on a Pi, connect a remote AI worker via gRPC. |
152	
153	### Section 7: Enterprise Ready
154	
155	**Headline**: "Built for teams that don't compromise."
156	
157	**Layout**: 6 cards in a 3x2 grid (stacks on mobile)
158	
159	| Card | Copy |
160	|------|------|
161	| Multi-Tenant Isolation | Full Row-Level Security per tenant in PostgreSQL. Your data never crosses boundaries. |
162	| SSO / SCIM | Keycloak-powered OIDC and SAML out of the box. SCIM provisioning coming soon. |
163	| Audit Logs | Timestamped audit trail for every action. *(Coming soon — in roadmap)* |
164	| BYOK | Bring your own LLM keys — Anthropic, OpenAI, Cohere, or self-hosted models. Zero vendor lock-in. |
165	| eBPF WAF | Kernel-level XDP pre-filtering and observability at the edge. |
166	| GDPR / SOC2 | Built for compliance. Self-host for full data sovereignty. |
