206	
207	## Technical Implementation
208	
209	### Stack
210	- **Framework**: Static HTML + Tailwind CSS v4 (paid license — Tailwind Plus/UI components)
211	- **Build**: Tailwind CLI with purging for production (NOT the CDN play script — must meet <500KB target)
212	- **Theme**: Tailwind `dark:` class variant, toggled via JavaScript on `<html>` element, default from `prefers-color-scheme`
213	- **Chat Widget**: Embedded `<raven-chat>` web component (mock mode at launch, live API post-launch)
214	- **Hosting**: Static site at raven.ravencloak.org (Cloudflare Pages)
215	- **Responsive**: Mobile-first, breakpoints at `sm`, `md`, `lg`, `xl`
216	- **Font loading**: Inter via Google Fonts with `font-display: swap`
217	
218	### SEO & Meta Tags
219	
220	```html
221	<title>Raven — The AI Brain for Your Entire Team</title>
222	<meta name="description" content="Open-source AI knowledge platform. Ingest your docs, ask in natural language, get cited answers via chat, voice, or WhatsApp. Self-host or use our cloud.">
223	<meta property="og:title" content="Raven — Open Source AI Knowledge Platform">
224	<meta property="og:description" content="Ingest your docs. Ask in natural language. Get cited answers via chat, voice, or WhatsApp.">
225	<meta property="og:url" content="https://raven.ravencloak.org">
226	<meta property="og:image" content="https://raven.ravencloak.org/og-image.png">
227	<meta property="og:type" content="website">
228	<meta name="twitter:card" content="summary_large_image">
229	<link rel="canonical" href="https://raven.ravencloak.org">
230	```
231	
232	OG image: 1200x630px, Raven logo + tagline on dark background. Created as a static asset.
233	
234	### Domain Map
235	
236	| Purpose | Domain |
237	|---------|--------|
238	| Marketing/landing page | `raven.ravencloak.org` |
239	| Application (dashboard) | `app.ravencloak.org` |
240	| API server | `api.ravencloak.org` |
241	| Auth (Keycloak) | `auth.ravencloak.org` |
242	| Documentation | `docs.ravencloak.org` (future) |
243	
244	### Accessibility
245	
246	- Amber accent (`#fbbf24`) on black background: contrast ratio ~11.6:1 (passes WCAG AA and AAA)
247	- Muted text (`#737373`) on black: contrast ratio ~4.6:1 (passes AA for normal text)
248	- All interactive elements: visible focus ring (`ring-2 ring-amber-400 ring-offset-2 ring-offset-black`)
249	- `prefers-reduced-motion`: disable scroll animations
250	- Semantic HTML: `<nav>`, `<main>`, `<section>`, `<footer>` with `aria-label` on each section
251	
252	### Interactions & Animations
253	
254	- **Scroll animations**: Subtle fade-in-up on section entry (via `IntersectionObserver`), respects `prefers-reduced-motion`
255	- **Tab switching** (Section 4): Click to switch, amber underline slides to active tab, screenshot crossfades. On mobile: tabs become a vertical accordion
256	- **Hover states**: Cards lift with `hover:-translate-y-1 hover:border-amber-400/50` transition
257	- **Theme toggle**: Smooth color transition via `transition-colors duration-300` on `<html>`
258	- **GitHub stars**: Static count at build time (hardcoded, updated periodically) — avoids GitHub API rate limits
259	
260	### Assets Required
261	- Raven logo (SVG, themed — black and white variants)
262	- Product screenshots for Chat, Voice, WhatsApp features (from existing admin dashboard)
263	- Icons for deployment options and enterprise features (Tailwind Heroicons or similar)
264	
265	### Demo Knowledge Base
266	- A curated knowledge base containing Raven's own documentation
267	- Hosted on the production API or a dedicated demo instance
268	- The `<raven-chat>` widget connects via a domain-scoped API key
269	- Rate-limited for public access (anonymous users, read-only)
270	
271	### Performance Targets
272	- First Contentful Paint < 1.5s
273	- Total page weight < 500KB (excluding chat widget)
274	- Lighthouse score > 90 across all categories
275	
276	## Prerequisites (must resolve before implementation)
277	
278	1. **Domain setup**: `raven.ravencloak.org` DNS must be configured and pointing to Cloudflare Pages (or chosen hosting)
279	2. **Raven logo**: Finalized SVG logo in black and white variants — if none exists, create a minimal text/icon logo as placeholder
280	
281	## Open Questions (non-blocking, can resolve during implementation)
282	
283	1. **Product screenshots**: Can we capture from the existing admin dashboard, or do we mock them? (Fallback: styled placeholder illustrations)
284	2. **OG image**: Design and generate the 1200x630 social sharing image
285	3. **GitHub stars count**: Current count to hardcode at build time
286	
287	## Out of Scope
288	
289	- User authentication / sign-up flow (handled by app.ravencloak.org)
290	- Blog / changelog pages (future addition)
291	- Documentation site (separate docs.ravencloak.org)
292	- A/B testing / analytics integration (PostHog — future addition)
293

Now I have all the information needed for a thorough review. Let me compile my findings.

---

## Review: Plan vs. Spec for Raven Landing Page

### MUST-FIX Issues (Blocking)

**1. `tailwind.config.js` listed in file structure contradicts Tailwind v4**

In the File Structure block (line 33), the plan lists `tailwind.config.js` with the comment "not needed for v4, but kept for reference." This file should not be listed at all. Tailwind CSS v4 does not use `tailwind.config.js` -- configuration is done entirely in CSS via `@theme` directives in `input.css`. Including it, even "for reference," will confuse the implementing agent. The plan's own Task 1 Step 2 correctly uses `@import "tailwindcss"` and `@theme` in CSS. The file structure should remove the `tailwind.config.js` entry entirely.

**File:** `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-04-13-raven-landing-page.md`, line 33.

---

**2. Missing Tailwind v4 content detection configuration**

Task 1 Step 2 creates `input.css` with only `@import "tailwindcss"` and a `@theme` block for the font. Tailwind v4 CLI auto-detects content sources from the current working directory, but the plan's build command runs from `landing/` while the HTML file is at `landing/index.html`. This *should* work since v4 auto-scans, but the plan gives no indication that the implementer should verify this. More critically, the `@theme` block is incomplete -- it only defines `--font-sans` but does not extend any amber color tokens or other custom theme values. Since Tailwind v4's default palette already includes amber, this technically works, but the plan should explicitly note this relies on built-in colors rather than leaving it ambiguous.

---

**3. Spec Section 2 calls for `<raven-chat>` web component; plan substitutes with hardcoded HTML mockup but never adds the real widget**

The spec explicitly states the hero should have an "Embedded `<raven-chat>` web component" running "in mock mode with curated hardcoded Q&A pairs" at Phase 1 launch. The plan's Task 4 Step 2 builds a static HTML mockup instead. This is a reasonable Phase 1 approach, but the plan has **no task** for loading the actual `<raven-chat>` web component script (the spec says "The `<raven-chat>` widget JS is loaded from the frontend build"). The file structure notes at line 40 say "The `<raven-chat>` widget JS is loaded from the frontend build, not duplicated here" but no task adds a `<script>` tag for it. Either:
- A task should be added to wire in the real web component (even if mock-mode), or
- The plan should explicitly state the hardcoded mockup IS the Phase 1 deliverable and add a TODO/follow-up item for the real widget.

As written, there is a gap between spec and plan.

---

**4. Spec section count mismatch with plan's HTML skeleton**

Task 2 Step 1 (line 127) says the `<main>` should have "9 empty `<section>` placeholders." The spec defines sections 2-9 as content inside `<main>` (hero, problem, features, how-it-works, deploy, enterprise, pricing, bottom CTA) = 8 sections. Section 1 (nav) is `<nav>`, Section 10 (footer) is `<footer>`. The plan says 10 sections in the File Structure comment (line 19: "all 10 sections") but 9 `<section>` tags in `<main>`. The correct count of `<section>` elements inside `<main>` should be **8** (sections 2-9 from the spec), since the bottom CTA (section 9 in spec) could be either a `<section>` or part of the footer area. This discrepancy will confuse the implementer on how many placeholders to create.

---

**5. No `@source` directive for Tailwind v4 CLI content scanning**

Tailwind CSS v4 CLI automatically scans for content, but when `input.css` is in `landing/src/` and it builds with `-i src/input.css -o dist/output.css`, the working directory is `landing/`. The v4 CLI scans from the location of the input CSS file (`landing/src/`) by default, not from the working directory. This means `landing/index.html` might not be detected. The plan should add an explicit `@source "../"` directive in `input.css` to ensure the HTML file is included in content detection, or verify the exact v4 CLI behavior. Without this, the production build may produce empty CSS with no utilities.

**File:** `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-04-13-raven-landing-page.md`, Task 1 Step 2 (line 69-75).

---

**6. Existing landing page uses CDN Tailwind play script -- plan does not mention removing it**

The current `landing/index.html` (line 15) loads `https://cdn.tailwindcss.com` and uses a JS-based `tailwind.config` object with an indigo/purple color scheme. Task 2 says "replace entirely" which handles this, but the plan should note that NO CDN script should remain in the new file. The spec explicitly says "NOT the CDN play script -- must meet <500KB target." This is implicit in "replace entirely" but worth an explicit verification step since accidentally keeping the CDN script would double-load Tailwind and break the performance target.

---

### Important Issues (Should Fix)

**7. Asset creation tasks (Task 13) are sequenced too late**

Task 13 creates placeholder assets (logos, screenshots, OG image) AFTER all content sections are already built (Tasks 3-11). Tasks 3-11 reference logos and screenshots but use text placeholders. This means the implementer builds all sections with placeholders, then rewires them in Task 13. This is workable but creates unnecessary rework. A better sequence: create placeholder assets in Task 2 (or as a parallel Task 2b) so all subsequent tasks can reference real file paths from the start.

---

**8. No `prefers-reduced-motion` handling for theme transition**

Task 2 Step 1 puts `transition-colors duration-300` on the `<body>`. Task 12 adds `prefers-reduced-motion` handling for scroll animations, but not for the body transition. The spec's accessibility section mentions respecting reduced motion. The body-level color transition should also be disabled under `prefers-reduced-motion`.

---

**9. Light theme text in hero demo widget has a bug**

Task 4 Step 2 specifies `bg-neutral-950 dark:bg-neutral-950 light:bg-neutral-100` for the chat card. There is no `light:` variant in Tailwind CSS. The correct approach is `bg-neutral-100 dark:bg-neutral-950` (light-first, with dark override). This will render incorrectly in light mode.

**File:** `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-04-13-raven-landing-page.md`, line 215.

---

**10. Plan mentions Heroicons by name but never explains how to include them**

Tasks 3, 5, 6, 8, and 9 reference Heroicons (inline SVG). The plan never explains whether these should be manually copied SVG markup, downloaded from the Heroicons website, or installed via npm. For a static HTML page, inline SVG is the correct approach, but the plan should state explicitly: "Copy SVG markup from heroicons.com for each icon" so the implementer does not try to install `@heroicons/react` or similar.

---

**11. Mobile accordion in Task 6 Step 4 is underspecified**

The plan says to convert tabs to an accordion on mobile using `<details>/<summary>` or CSS class toggling, but provides no code or detailed structure. Given that the tab JS in Step 3 uses `data-tab-trigger`/`data-tab-panel` attributes, the accordion would need separate markup or significant conditional logic. This step needs more guidance -- specifically whether to use two separate markup structures (tabs hidden on mobile, accordion hidden on desktop) or a single adaptive structure.

---

**12. Missing skip-to-content link for accessibility**

The spec mentions semantic HTML with `aria-label` on sections, and the plan follows this. However, neither the spec nor the plan includes a skip-to-content link (`<a href="#main-content" class="sr-only focus:not-sr-only">Skip to content</a>`), which is a basic WCAG requirement for keyboard navigation. This would hurt the Lighthouse accessibility score target of >90.

---

### Nice-to-Have Improvements

**13. Tailwind Plus component references are realistic but could be more specific**

The plan references Tailwind Plus categories (Marketing > Headers, Marketing > Hero Sections, etc.) which is correct. However, Tailwind Plus components are React/Vue by default. Since this is a static HTML page, the plan should note that the implementer needs to extract the HTML/CSS from these components and strip any framework-specific markup (no `className`, no JSX). This is implied but worth stating.

---

**14. No CI/build integration mentioned**

The plan creates a standalone `landing/package.json` with build scripts but does not mention integrating with the project's CI pipeline. If the landing page should be built in CI (for Cloudflare Pages deployment), a task or note should cover this. This is not blocking for initial development but will be needed before deployment.

---

**15. Commit granularity is good but could batch better**

14 tasks with individual commits is reasonable, but Tasks 5-9 (content sections) are small and independent. They could be batched into fewer commits without losing clarity. This is stylistic and non-blocking.

---

**16. GitHub stars badge count source**

Task 3 mentions a "hardcoded star count badge" for the GitHub link. The spec says "Static count at build time (hardcoded, updated periodically)." The plan does not specify where to get the current count or how to update it. A small note like "check github.com/ravencloak/raven for current count" would help.

---

### Summary

| Category | Count | Key Items |
|----------|-------|-----------|
| MUST-FIX | 6 | Remove `tailwind.config.js` from file tree; add `@source` directive for v4 CLI content scanning; fix `light:` variant to standard Tailwind; clarify section count; address `<raven-chat>` widget gap; note CDN removal |
| Important | 6 | Reorder asset creation; fix `prefers-reduced-motion` for transitions; specify Heroicons usage; flesh out mobile accordion; add skip-to-content link; fix light theme class |
| Nice-to-have | 4 | Note Tailwind Plus HTML extraction; CI integration; commit batching; GitHub stars source |

The plan is well-structured overall with good task sequencing, correct Tailwind v4 entry point syntax (`@import "tailwindcss"`), and faithful coverage of all 10 spec sections. The primary risks are the `@source` directive omission (which could produce empty CSS in production builds) and the `light:` variant typo (which will break light theme rendering).

277	### Task 6: Tabbed Features Section
278	
279	**Files:**
280	- Modify: `landing/index.html` (populate features `<section>`)
281	
282	This requires **custom tab JS** — no direct Tailwind Plus drop-in for interactive tabs. Use Tailwind Plus **Feature Sections** layout for each tab's content, and **Application UI > Tabs** for the tab bar styling.
283	
284	- [ ] **Step 1: Build the tab bar**
285	
286	3 tabs: "AI Chat", "Voice Agent", "WhatsApp". Active tab has `border-b-2 border-amber-400 text-amber-400` indicator. Inactive tabs: `text-neutral-500 hover:text-neutral-300`.
287	
288	- [ ] **Step 2: Build the 3 tab panels**
289	
290	Each panel (initially hidden except first):
291	- Split layout: screenshot placeholder on one side (a styled `div` with `bg-neutral-900 rounded-xl aspect-video` and centered text "Screenshot: [feature name]"), description on the other
292	- Headline, copy, and a small "Learn more →" link
293	- Wrap in a container with `data-tab="chat|voice|whatsapp"` attribute
294	
295	- [ ] **Step 3: Add tab switching JavaScript**
296	
297	```javascript
298	document.querySelectorAll('[data-tab-trigger]').forEach(tab => {
299	  tab.addEventListener('click', () => {
300	    // Remove active from all triggers
301	    document.querySelectorAll('[data-tab-trigger]').forEach(t => {
302	      t.classList.remove('border-amber-400', 'text-amber-400')
303	      t.classList.add('text-neutral-500')
304	    })
305	    // Add active to clicked
306	    tab.classList.add('border-amber-400', 'text-amber-400')
307	    tab.classList.remove('text-neutral-500')
308	    // Show matching panel, hide others
309	    const target = tab.dataset.tabTrigger
310	    document.querySelectorAll('[data-tab-panel]').forEach(p => {
311	      p.classList.toggle('hidden', p.dataset.tabPanel !== target)
312	    })
313	  })
314	})
315	```
316	
317	- [ ] **Step 4: Add mobile accordion behavior**
318	
319	On screens below `md:`, convert tabs to a vertical stack where each tab heading toggles its content panel open/closed (accordion pattern). Use a `<details>/<summary>` approach or CSS class toggling.
320	
321	- [ ] **Step 5: Test tabs**
322	
323	Expected: Desktop — 3 horizontal tabs, click switches content with correct highlight. Mobile — accordion with toggleable panels.
324

496	### Task 12: Scroll Animations & Polish
497	
498	**Files:**
499	- Modify: `landing/index.html` (add animation JS and final CSS)
500	- Modify: `landing/src/input.css` (add animation keyframes if needed)
501	
502	- [ ] **Step 1: Add scroll-triggered fade-in animations**
503	
504	```javascript
505	const observer = new IntersectionObserver((entries) => {
506	  entries.forEach(entry => {
507	    if (entry.isIntersecting) {
508	      entry.target.classList.add('animate-fade-in-up')
509	      observer.unobserve(entry.target)
510	    }
511	  })
512	}, { threshold: 0.1 })
513	
514	document.querySelectorAll('[data-animate]').forEach(el => {
515	  el.classList.add('opacity-0', 'translate-y-4')
516	  observer.observe(el)
517	})
518	```
519	
520	Add `data-animate` attribute to each section's content container.
521	
522	Add to `input.css`:
523	```css
524	@keyframes fade-in-up {
525	  from { opacity: 0; transform: translateY(1rem); }
526	  to { opacity: 1; transform: translateY(0); }
527	}
528	
529	.animate-fade-in-up {
530	  animation: fade-in-up 0.6s ease-out forwards;
531	}
532	
533	@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
534	  .animate-fade-in-up { animation: none; opacity: 1; transform: none; }
535	  [data-animate] { opacity: 1 !important; transform: none !important; }
536	}
537	```
538

541	Ensure all interactive elements (buttons, links, tabs) have visible focus ring: `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-amber-400 focus-visible:ring-offset-2 focus-visible:ring-offset-black dark:focus-visible:ring-offset-black`

  "new_string": "```css\n/* landing/src/input.css */\n@import \"tailwindcss\";\n@source \"../\";\n\n@theme {\n  --font-sans: \"Inter\", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif;\n}\n```\n\nThe `@source \"../\"` directive tells Tailwind v4 CLI to scan `landing/` (parent of `src/`) for class usage in HTML files. Without this, the CLI scans from `landing/src/` and may miss `index.html`. Tailwind v4's default palette already includes all amber colors, so no custom color tokens are needed."

  "new_string": "- [ ] **Step 4: Add mobile accordion behavior**\n\nUse **two separate markup structures**: the tab bar (hidden on mobile via `hidden md:flex`) and a `<details>/<summary>` accordion (hidden on desktop via `md:hidden`). Each `<details>` element contains the same content as its corresponding tab panel. This avoids complex conditional JS — CSS handles the switch:\n\n```html\n<!-- Desktop tabs (hidden on mobile) -->\n<div class=\"hidden md:flex border-b border-neutral-800 gap-8\">\n  <button data-tab-trigger=\"chat\" class=\"...\">AI Chat</button>\n  <!-- ... -->\n</div>\n<div class=\"hidden md:block\">\n  <div data-tab-panel=\"chat\"><!-- content --></div>\n  <!-- ... -->\n</div>\n\n<!-- Mobile accordion (hidden on desktop) -->\n<div class=\"md:hidden space-y-4\">\n  <details open>\n    <summary class=\"cursor-pointer font-semibold text-lg py-3 border-b border-neutral-800\">AI Chat</summary>\n    <div class=\"py-4\"><!-- same content --></div>\n  </details>\n  <!-- ... -->\n</div>\n```"
}

28	
29	    ## Your Job
30	
31	    Once you're clear on requirements:
32	    1. Implement exactly what the task specifies
33	    2. Write tests (following TDD if task says to)
34	    3. Verify implementation works
35	    4. Commit your work
36	    5. Self-review (see below)
37	    6. Report back
38	
39	    Work from: [directory]
40	
41	    **While you work:** If you encounter something unexpected or unclear, **ask questions**.
42	    It's always OK to pause and clarify. Don't guess or make assumptions.
43	
44	    ## Code Organization
45	
46	    You reason best about code you can hold in context at once, and your edits are more
47	    reliable when files are focused. Keep this in mind:
48	    - Follow the file structure defined in the plan
49	    - Each file should have one clear responsibility with a well-defined interface
50	    - If a file you're creating is growing beyond the plan's intent, stop and report
51	      it as DONE_WITH_CONCERNS — don't split files on your own without plan guidance
52	    - If an existing file you're modifying is already large or tangled, work carefully
53	      and note it as a concern in your report
54	    - In existing codebases, follow established patterns. Improve code you're touching
55	      the way a good developer would, but don't restructure things outside your task.
56	
57	    ## When You're in Over Your Head
58	
59	    It is always OK to stop and say "this is too hard for me." Bad work is worse than
60	    no work. You will not be penalized for escalating.
61	
62	    **STOP and escalate when:**
63	    - The task requires architectural decisions with multiple valid approaches
64	    - You need to understand code beyond what was provided and can't find clarity
65	    - You feel uncertain about whether your approach is correct
66	    - The task involves restructuring existing code in ways the plan didn't anticipate
67	    - You've been reading file after file trying to understand the system without progress
68	
69	    **How to escalate:** Report back with status BLOCKED or NEEDS_CONTEXT. Describe
70	    specifically what you're stuck on, what you've tried, and what kind of help you need.
71	    The controller can provide more context, re-dispatch with a more capable model,
72	    or break the task into smaller pieces.
73	
74	    ## Before Reporting Back: Self-Review
75	
76	    Review your work with fresh eyes. Ask yourself:
77	
78	    **Completeness:**
79	    - Did I fully implement everything in the spec?
80	    - Did I miss any requirements?
81	    - Are there edge cases I didn't handle?
82	
83	    **Quality:**
84	    - Is this my best work?
85	    - Are names clear and accurate (match what things do, not how they work)?
86	    - Is the code clean and maintainable?
87	
88	    **Discipline:**
89	    - Did I avoid overbuilding (YAGNI)?
90	    - Did I only build what was requested?
91	    - Did I follow existing patterns in the codebase?
92	
93	    **Testing:**
94	    - Do tests actually verify behavior (not just mock behavior)?
95	    - Did I follow TDD if required?
96	    - Are tests comprehensive?
97	
98	    If you find issues during self-review, fix them now before reporting.
99	
100	    ## Report Format
101	
102	    When done, report:
103	    - **Status:** DONE | DONE_WITH_CONCERNS | BLOCKED | NEEDS_CONTEXT
104	    - What you implemented (or what you attempted, if blocked)
105	    - What you tested and test results
106	    - Files changed
107	    - Self-review findings (if any)
108	    - Any issues or concerns
109	
110	    Use DONE_WITH_CONCERNS if you completed the work but have doubts about correctness.
111	    Use BLOCKED if you cannot complete the task. Use NEEDS_CONTEXT if you need
112	    information that wasn't provided. Never silently produce work you're unsure about.

## THOROUGH EXPLORATION: Tailwind Plus Radiant Template

I've completed a comprehensive analysis of the Tailwind Plus Radiant template. Here's the complete breakdown you need for the Raven landing page implementation:

---

### **1. PROJECT STRUCTURE & BUILD SETUP**

**Stack:**
- **Framework**: Next.js 16.1.6 (App Router)
- **Styling**: Tailwind CSS v4.2.2 (uses `@tailwindcss/postcss`)
- **React**: 19.2.4
- **Animation**: Framer Motion 12.23.11
- **UI Components**: Headless UI 2.2.6 + Heroicons 2.2.0
- **Font**: Switzer (loaded via Fontshare API)
- **CMS**: Sanity (integrated but optional)

**Directory Structure:**
```
radiant-js/
├── src/
│   ├── app/
│   │   ├── page.jsx (home/marketing)
│   │   ├── pricing/page.jsx
│   │   ├── company/page.jsx
│   │   ├── blog/ (blog pages)
│   │   ├── layout.jsx (root layout with fonts & metadata)
│   │   └── studio/ (Sanity CMS)
│   ├── components/ (17 reusable components)
│   ├── styles/tailwind.css (minimal - just @import)
│   └── sanity/ (CMS config - can be removed)
├── package.json
├── tailwind.config.js (not found - uses v4 default config)
├── postcss.config.js
└── next.config.mjs (empty - basic Next config)
```

**Key Insight**: This is a **Tailwind v4 project** using the new PostCSS integration. No `tailwind.config.js` exists because v4 uses a `@theme` block in CSS and doesn't require external config.

---

### **2. TAILWIND & DESIGN SYSTEM**

**Color Palette:**
- **Grays**: `gray-50` through `gray-950` (standard)
- **Accent Gradient**: `#fff1be` (yellow) → `#ee87cb` (pink) → `#b060ff` (purple)
- **Dark Mode Color**: `bg-gray-900` and `bg-gray-800` for dark sections
- **Ring Colors**: `ring-black/5`, `ring-white/15` (alpha variants heavily used)
- **Gradients**: Linear gradients at angles 115° and 145° for hero backgrounds

**Typography:**
- **Font Family**: Switzer (loaded in layout.jsx from Fontshare)
- **Font Weights**: 400, 500, 600, 700
- **Heading Sizes**: 
  - `h1`: `text-8xl` (sm: 6xl, base: 6xl) - tight line-height `[0.8]` or `[0.9]`
  - `h2`: `text-4xl` → `text-6xl` depending on component
  - `h3`: `text-2xl`
  - `Subheading`: `text-xs/5 font-semibold uppercase` (monospace-looking)

**Spacing & Layout:**
- `Container` component: `px-6 lg:px-8` + `mx-auto max-w-2xl lg:max-w-7xl`
- Common gaps: `gap-4`, `gap-6`, `gap-8`
- Padding: `p-10`, `p-6`, `px-3 py-0.5` (small pills)

**Shadows & Borders:**
- `shadow-xs`, `shadow-md`, `shadow-2xl` (heavily used)
- `ring-1 ring-black/5` (common outline pattern)
- `rounded-4xl` (large cards), `rounded-3xl` (medium), `rounded-lg` (small)

**Custom Features:**
- `@theme` block in CSS defines custom radius: `--radius-4xl: 2rem`
- Animation: `@keyframes move-x` for horizontal scrolling logos
- CSS variables like `--spacing()`, `--container-2xl`, `--scroll-padding`

---

### **3. COMPONENT INVENTORY**

| Component | Purpose | Key Features |
|-----------|---------|-------------|
| **navbar.jsx** | Header with mobile menu | Disclosure UI, animated mobile nav (rotateX), Headless UI, logo + banner |
| **button.jsx** | CTA buttons | 3 variants: primary (dark), secondary (glass), outline. Uses `data-hover` and `data-disabled` states |
| **container.jsx** | Layout wrapper | Max-width container with responsive padding |
| **text.jsx** | Heading/Subheading | Heading: `text-gray-950` with `data-dark:text-white`. Subheading: uppercase, monospace |
| **bento-card.jsx** | Feature card | Grid item with image + text. Uses motion.div with hover variants. Fade overlays (top/bottom) |
| **gradient.jsx** | Gradient background | Pre-built gradient: yellow→pink→purple at 115°/145° angles |
| **logo-cloud.jsx** | Logo row | Responsive grid of 5 logos, flexes on mobile |
| **logo-timeline.jsx** | Animated logo ticker | Horizontal scrolling logos with animation delays, masks center |
| **logo-cluster.jsx** | Orbiting logos | Central logo with 6 surrounding logos that animate on hover using Motion variants |
| **plus-grid.jsx** | Grid system | Custom grid with decorative "+" corners and borders. PlusGrid, PlusGridRow, PlusGridItem |
| **screenshot.jsx** | Image container | Aspect-ratio wrapper with shadow/ring styling |
| **map.jsx** | Animated markers | Motion markers dropping onto map image. Bouncy animation |
| **linked-avatars.jsx** | Avatar connection | Animated rings + 2 avatars + checkmark. Complex motion setup |
| **keyboard.jsx** | Keyboard visualizer | Individual keys with highlight glow animation. Context-based |
| **testimonials.jsx** | Carousel section | Horizontal scroll, snap points, opacity based on position. Active indicator dots |
| **footer.jsx** | Footer | Sitemap grid + social icons + copyright. Uses PlusGrid layout |
| **link.jsx** | Link wrapper | Headless UI DataInteractive + Next Link |
| **logo.jsx** | SVG logo | Animated Radiant mark with motion groups |
| **animated-number.jsx** | Number counter | Framer Motion spring animation on scroll into view |

---

### **4. PAGE STRUCTURE MAPPING**

#### **Home Page (page.jsx) Sections:**

1. **Hero Section**
   - Large gradient background
   - H1: "Close every deal." (9xl text)
   - Subheading text (xl, 75% opacity)
   - 2 buttons (Get started, See pricing)
   - Navbar with banner link
   - Uses: `Gradient`, `Navbar`, `Button`, `Container`

2. **Logo Cloud Section**
   - 5 company logos in a row
   - Responsive: wraps on mobile
   - Use: `LogoCloud`

3. **Feature Screenshot Section**
   - Full-width app screenshot with rounded corners
   - Shadow and ring styling
   - Use: `Screenshot`

4. **Bento Grid Section (Light)**
   - 6 cards in asymmetric grid (3-2-1 layout on lg)
   - Cards: Insight, Analysis, Speed, Source, Limitless (5 cards visible, last spans 2)
   - Each has: eyebrow label, title, description, graphic
   - Fade overlays on images
   - Uses: `BentoCard` (multiple), `Keyboard`, `LogoCluster`, `Map`

5. **Dark Bento Section (bg-gray-900)**
   - 4 cards with dark styling
   - Cards: Networking, Integrations, Meetings, Engagement
   - Uses: `BentoCard` (dark variant), `LogoTimeline`, `LinkedAvatars`

6. **Testimonials Section**
   - Horizontal scrolling carousel
   - 6 testimonial cards with images + quotes
   - Active dot indicators
   - Uses: `Testimonials` component

7. **Footer**
   - CTA section
   - Sitemap grid
   - Social links
   - Uses: `Footer`

#### **Pricing Page (pricing/page.jsx):**
- Header + title
- 3 pricing cards (Starter $99, Growth $149, Enterprise $299)
- Comparison table (responsive dropdown on mobile)
- Testimonial with image
- FAQs section
- Uses: `Heading`, `Button`, `Container`, `PricingCard` (custom)

#### **Company Page (company/page.jsx):**
- Hero + mission statement
- Photo grid
- "The Numbers" section with animated counters
- Team grid (9 people)
- Investors section
- Careers table
- Uses: `AnimatedNumber`, `Heading`, `Person` components

---

### **5. KEY TAILWIND PATTERNS & TECHNIQUES**

**Responsive Design:**
```jsx
// Mobile-first defaults, then overrides
className="px-6 lg:px-8"  // 6 on mobile, 8 on lg
className="max-sm:flex-wrap max-sm:justify-evenly"  // Mobile specific
className="hidden lg:flex"  // Hidden on mobile, shown on lg
```

**Data Attributes for Styling:**
```jsx
data-dark={dark ? 'true' : undefined}
className="... text-gray-950 data-dark:text-white"
data-hover:bg-black/2.5  // Headless UI hover state
data-disabled:opacity-40  // Headless UI disabled state
data-active:bg-gray-400  // Custom active state
```

**Aspect Ratios & Sizing:**
```jsx
className="aspect-[var(--width)/var(--height)]"  // Dynamic aspect
className="aspect-square"  // 1:1
className="aspect-3/4"  // Photo aspect
className="size-4"  // width AND height (shorthand)
```

**Gradients:**
```jsx
className="bg-linear-to-r from-[#fff1be] from-28% via-[#ee87cb] via-70% to-[#b060ff]"
className="bg-linear-to-t from-black from-[calc(7/16*100%)]"  // Calc stops
className="bg-linear-115"  // Angle-based (v4 feature)
```

**Masks & Clipping:**
```jsx
style={{
  maskImage: `url('data:image/svg+xml,...')`,
  maskPosition: 'center',
  maskRepeat: 'no-repeat',
}}
```

**CSS Variables:**
```jsx
style={{ '--width': width, '--height': height }}
className="aspect-[var(--width)/var(--height)]"  // Used in size calculations
```

**Transform & Motion:**
```jsx
className="-translate-x-1/2 -translate-y-1/2"
className="rotate-[-10deg]"  // Negative rotation
className="group-hover:[animation-play-state:running]"  // Group hover + animation state
```

---

### **6. ANIMATION PATTERNS**

**Framer Motion Patterns:**

1. **Hover Scale Animation**
   ```jsx
   initial="idle"
   whileHover="active"
   variants={{
     idle: {},
     active: { scale: 1.15, ... }
   }}
   ```

2. **Scroll-triggered Animations**
   - `useInView` hook to detect when element enters viewport
   - `AnimatedNumber` example: counts up when visible

3. **Continuous Loops**
   ```jsx
   transition={{
     duration: 0.75,
     repeat: Infinity,
     repeatDelay: 1.25,
     ease: 'easeInOut'
   }}
   ```

4. **Staggered Animations**
   - Child elements animate sequentially with `delay` offsets
   - Logo cluster: each logo has different `hover.delay`

5. **Spring Animations**
   ```jsx
   useSpring(value, { damping: 30, stiffness: 100 })
   ```

6. **SVG Animations**
   - Logo: animated circles with `motion.circle`
   - Testimonial: opacity transitions based on scroll position

---

### **7. ACCESSIBILITY FEATURES**

- `aria-label` on buttons and links
- `aria-hidden="true"` on decorative elements (gradients, circles)
- `role="list"` on item lists
- `<caption>` on tables (`sr-only`)
- Semantic HTML: `<figure>`, `<figcaption>`, `<blockquote>`
- Focus states via Headless UI (`data-focus`)
- Forced color support for high contrast mode

---

### **8. DARK/LIGHT THEME SUPPORT**

**No system-level dark mode toggle**, but:
- Dark sections use `bg-gray-900` + `data-dark` attribute pattern
- BentoCard accepts `dark` prop for styling variants
- Text colors flip: `text-gray-950 data-dark:text-white`
- Ring colors update: `ring-black/5` → `ring-white/15` (dark)
- No CSS `@media (prefers-color-scheme)` detected

**For Raven**: You'll need to add a theme toggle if dark mode is required.

---

### **9. FONT & ASSET LOADING**

**Fonts (from layout.jsx):**
```jsx
<link
  rel="stylesheet"
  href="https://api.fontshare.com/css?f%5B%5D=switzer@400,500,600,700&display=swap"
/>
```

**Images:**
- `public/logo-cloud/` - 5 logos
- `public/screenshots/` - 5 app screenshots
- `public/team/` - 9 team photos
- `public/testimonials/` - 6 testimonial photos
- `public/company/` - 5 company gallery images
- `public/individual-investors/` - 6 investor photos

---

### **10. MAPPING RADIANT COMPONENTS → RAVEN SECTIONS**

| Raven Section | Radiant Component(s) | Pattern |
|---------------|---------------------|---------|
| **Navigation** | `navbar.jsx` | Desktop + mobile menu, logo, banner support |
| **Hero** | Home Hero section | Large h1, subtext, buttons, gradient background |
| **Problem Statement** | `FeatureSection` | Heading + full-width screenshot |
| **Tabbed Features** | `BentoSection` | 6-card asymmetric grid with titles/descriptions |
| **How It Works** | `DarkBentoSection` | Dark cards with 4 items + integrations logos |
| **Deploy Your Way** | Logo animations | `LogoTimeline` or `LogoCluster` |
| **Enterprise Ready** | Feature cards | `BentoCard` dark variant |
| **Pricing** | `pricing/page.jsx` | 3-tier cards + comparison table |
| **Bottom CTA** | Footer CallToAction | Centered CTA in `Footer` |
| **Footer** | `footer.jsx` | Sitemap, socials, copyright |
| **Scroll Effects** | Testimonials + animations | Framer Motion + scroll listeners |

---

### **11. NOTABLE PATTERNS FOR RAVEN**

**What to Reuse Directly:**
1. `navbar.jsx` - Drop-in navigation
2. `button.jsx` - CTA button system (all 3 variants useful)
3. `container.jsx` - Layout wrapper
4. `text.jsx` - Heading/Subheading system
5. `gradient.jsx` - Background accent element (adjust colors)
6. `plus-grid.jsx` - Decorative grid lines
7. `footer.jsx` - Footer structure (update links)

**What to Adapt:**

2. `testimonials.jsx` - Reuse scroll/snap carousel logic for any carousel

**What to Build New:**
1. Custom `tabbed-features.jsx` component (not in Radiant)
2. Pricing comparison table (Radiant has this but different layout)
3. "How It Works" timeline or step component

---

### **12. BUILD PERFORMANCE NOTES**

- **Bundling**: Uses Next.js default (Turbopack in dev)
- **Image Optimization**: `next/image` not used; raw `<img>` tags (can optimize)
- **CSS**: Tailwind v4 produces minimal CSS (only used utilities)
- **JS**: Framer Motion + Headless UI (no heavy libraries beyond that)
- **Fonts**: Google Fonts (external load) - consider self-hosting for Raven

> AGENT

Confirmed — Tailwind v4 is generating dark mode via `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` instead of the `.dark` class. That's why the theme toggle doesn't work. I need to override the dark variant to use class-based detection.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_018JxZMnQAVmzLfC9xWp9PbS
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/landing/src/input.css",
  "old_string": "/* landing/src/input.css */\n@import \"tailwindcss\";\n@source \"../\";\n\n@theme {\n  --font-sans: \"Inter\", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif;\n}",
  "new_string": "/* landing/src/input.css */\n@import \"tailwindcss\";\n@source \"../\";\n\n@custom-variant dark (&:where(.dark, .dark *));\n\n@theme {\n  --font-sans: \"Inter\", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif;\n}"
}

> AGENT

Fixed. Now rebuild and try again — **refresh http://localhost:3334**. The theme toggle should now work.

The key fix was adding `@custom-variant dark (&:where(.dark, .dark *));` to `input.css`. Tailwind v4 defaults to `prefers-color-scheme` media queries, but our theme toggle uses the `.dark` class on `<html>`, so we needed to override the variant.

The "Live Demo" chat widget is intentionally a static mockup (Phase 1) — it won't be interactive until the real SSE chat API is wired in. The tabs, smooth scroll, and mobile menu should all work now. Try refreshing.

**Stylelint must be configured to recognize Tailwind v4 at-rules to prevent CI failure.**

Lines 3, 5, and 7 contain valid Tailwind v4 directives (`@source`, `@custom-variant`, `@theme`), but the Stylelint config extends `stylelint-config-standard-scss` without allowlisting these at-rules. The `at-rule-no-unknown` rule will flag them as errors. Add the `@tailwindcss/stylelint` plugin to the Stylelint config, or explicitly disable `at-rule-no-unknown` for this file pattern.

Based on the comprehensive analysis from the previous session, I can see the critical path forward. The landing page "Get Started Free" button currently has nowhere to go—there's no public registration flow, no unauthenticated org creation, and the onboarding wizard has no API wiring.

Let me identify the exact minimal scope needed to make the signup→org→first query flow functional:

**Immediate blockers to resolve:**

1. **Registration endpoint** — Need to decide: modify Keycloak realm to allow self-registration, or build custom Go endpoint?
2. **First org creation** — POST /api/v1/orgs currently requires org_admin role (chicken-and-egg problem)
3. **Workspace auto-creation** — KB creation needs workspace_id but wizard doesn't handle this
4. **Onboarding API wiring** — 5 wizard steps currently return TODOs

**Questions to guide the implementation priority:**

- Should each organization get its own Keycloak realm (multi-tenant isolation), or share the "raven" realm with role-based access control?
- Should the landing page link to a custom registration form, or redirect to Keycloak's built-in registration UI?
- Should onboarding auto-create a default workspace, or require user selection?
- Is billing/free-tier auto-assignment required on day 1, or can it be deferred?

**What I can do now:**

I can generate the implementation for:
1. A public registration endpoint that handles user creation + org creation + workspace initialization in a single transaction
2. Complete the onboarding wizard API wiring with error handling
3. Add default workspace auto-creation logic
4. Wire the chat test (step 4) to actually call the chat API with a test KB

Would you like me to start with one of these, or do you want to clarify the architectural decisions (realm strategy, registration UI, etc.) first?

Before I start asking questions, let me flag the **scope**. This is actually **multiple independent subsystems**:

1. **User Registration** — Keycloak self-signup or custom endpoint
2. **Org/Workspace Provisioning** — first org + default workspace creation
3. **Onboarding Wizard API wiring** — connect the 5 frontend steps to real APIs
4. **Connector Setup** — chat widget, voice, WhatsApp per-org config
5. **Deployment** — getting app.ravencloak.org live with all services

Each of these could be its own spec → plan → implementation cycle. Trying to brainstorm all 5 together will produce a vague spec.

**My recommendation:** Focus on the **critical path** — the minimum needed so a user can click "Get Started Free" on the landing page and reach a working product. That's:

1. Registration (sign up)
2. Org + workspace auto-creation
3. Onboarding wizard wiring (at least steps 1-3)

Connectors and deployment can follow as separate specs.

Does this scoping feel right, or do you want to include more/less?

> AGENT

That's a **major architectural pivot** — Keycloak is deeply integrated across the codebase right now (keycloak-js in frontend, JWT validation in Go middleware, realm auto-provisioning in the onboarding handler, deploy configs). Replacing it with Zitadel touches everything.

This needs to be decomposed into at least **3 sub-projects**:

| # | Sub-project | Scope |
|---|-------------|-------|
| 1 | **Zitadel migration** | Replace Keycloak with Zitadel — backend JWT validation, frontend OIDC client, Docker Compose, deploy configs |
| 2 | **Google IDP + WebAuthn** | Configure Zitadel for direct Google redirect (no Zitadel UI shown) + passkey registration |
| 3 | **Registration → Org flow** | Post-signup org/workspace auto-creation, onboarding wizard wiring |

Let me start with the first clarifying question:

**Are you committed to replacing Keycloak entirely, or would you consider running Zitadel alongside it?** Context:

- **A) Full replacement** — rip out Keycloak, replace with Zitadel everywhere. Cleaner long-term, but touches ~15+ files across backend/frontend/deploy. Breaking change for any existing users.
- **B) Zitadel for new signups only** — keep Keycloak for existing admin/internal auth, add Zitadel as the public-facing identity provider for new user registration. More complex architecture but zero-risk migration.
- **C) Full replacement, phased** — swap to Zitadel in one shot but do it as sub-project 1 before touching anything else. Get Zitadel working with existing flows first, then add Google IDP + WebAuthn.

I'd recommend **C** — clean cut, but phased so each step is testable.

> DEVELOPER

c

> AGENT

Good. Full Zitadel replacement, phased. Let's brainstorm **sub-project 1: Zitadel migration** first.

Next question: **How is Zitadel deployed?**

- **A) Self-hosted via Docker** — add Zitadel container to your existing Docker Compose alongside PostgreSQL. You control everything, fits your data sovereignty story.
- **B) Zitadel Cloud** — use their managed service (free tier available). Faster to set up, but adds an external dependency.
- **C) Self-hosted on a separate VM** — dedicated Zitadel instance on its own EC2/server, accessed by both API and frontend.

Given your edge deployment requirements and "own your data" philosophy, I'd recommend **A** — self-hosted in Docker Compose alongside your existing stack. Zitadel uses its own PostgreSQL database (or can share one with separate schemas).

### Docker Compose Configuration for ZITADEL

Source: https://zitadel.com/docs/self-hosting/manage/configure/configure

Docker Compose configuration to set up ZITADEL and its PostgreSQL database. It mounts example configuration files and specifies ports and volumes.

```yaml
services:
  zitadel:
    restart: "always"
    networks:
      - "zitadel"
    image: "ghcr.io/zitadel/zitadel:latest"
    command: 'start-from-init --config /example-zitadel-config.yaml --config /example-zitadel-secrets.yaml --steps /example-zitadel-init-steps.yaml --masterkey "${ZITADEL_MASTERKEY}" --tlsMode disabled'
    ports:
      - "8080:8080"
    volumes:
      - "./example-zitadel-config.yaml:/example-zitadel-config.yaml:ro"
      - "./example-zitadel-secrets.yaml:/example-zitadel-secrets.yaml:ro"
      - "./example-zitadel-init-steps.yaml:/example-zitadel-init-steps.yaml:ro"
    depends_on:
      db:
        condition: "service_healthy"

  db:
    image: postgres:17-alpine
    restart: always
    environment:
      - POSTGRES_USER=root
      - POSTGRES_PASSWORD=postgres
      - POSTGRES_DB=zitadel
    networks:
      - 'zitadel'
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD-SHELL", "pg_isready -d zitadel -U root"]
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 30s
      retries: 5
      start_period: 20s
    volumes:
      - 'data:/var/lib/postgresql/data:rw'

networks:
  zitadel:

volumes:
  data:


```

--------------------------------

### Start Database with Docker Compose

Source: https://zitadel.com/docs/self-hosting/manage/cli/mirror

Download the example configuration and start the database and application containers.

```bash
# Download the docker compose example configuration.
wget https://raw.githubusercontent.com/zitadel/zitadel/main/docs/self-hosting/deploy/docker-compose.yaml

# Run the database and application containers.
docker compose up db --detach
```

### Configure PostgreSQL Connection in Configuration File

Source: https://zitadel.com/docs/self-hosting/manage/production

Define PostgreSQL database connection parameters and connection pool limits within the ZITADEL configuration file. This method allows for centralized configuration management.

```yaml
Database:
  postgres:
    DSN: postgresql://zitadel:password@localhost:5432/zitadel?sslmode=disable
    //highlight-start
    MaxOpenConns: 10
    MaxIdleConns: 5
    MaxConnLifetime: 30m
    MaxConnIdleTime: 5m
    //highlight-end
```

--------------------------------

### Set PostgreSQL Connection via Environment Variables

Source: https://zitadel.com/docs/self-hosting/manage/production

Configure the PostgreSQL database connection details and connection pool settings using environment variables. Ensure SSL mode is appropriately set for your environment.

```bash
export ZITADEL_DATABASE_POSTGRES_DSN=postgresql://zitadel:password@localhost:5432/zitadel?sslmode=disable
export ZITADEL_DATABASE_POSTGRES_MAXOPENCONNS=10
export ZITADEL_DATABASE_POSTGRES_MAXIDLECONNS=5
export ZITADEL_DATABASE_POSTGRES_MAXCONNLIFETIME=30m
export ZITADEL_DATABASE_POSTGRES_MAXCONNIDLETIME=5m
```

### Self-Hosting > Manage > Configure > example-zitadel-config.yaml

Source: https://zitadel.com/docs/self-hosting/manage/configure/configure

The `example-zitadel-config.yaml` file allows setting logging levels and external security (HTTP vs. HTTPS). It also defines connection parameters for the PostgreSQL database, including host, port, database name, user credentials, and SSL mode. These settings are crucial if not using the Docker Compose example.

### POST /v2/idp_intents

Source: https://zitadel.com/docs/guides/integrate/login-ui/external-login

Initiates the authentication flow with an external identity provider.

```APIDOC
## POST /v2/idp_intents

### Description
Initiates the authentication flow with a configured external identity provider. Returns an authentication URL to redirect the user.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
https://${CUSTOM_DOMAIN}/v2/idp_intents

### Request Body
- **idpId** (string) - Required - The ID of the identity provider.
- **urls** (object) - Required - Contains successUrl and failureUrl.

### Request Example
{
  "idpId": "$IDP_ID",
  "urls": {
    "successUrl": "https://custom.com/login/idp/success",
    "failureUrl": "https://custom.com/login/idp/fail"
  }
}

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **details** (object) - Metadata about the intent creation.
- **authUrl** (string) - The URL to redirect the user to for external authentication.

#### Response Example
{
	"details": {
		"sequence": "592",
		"changeDate": "2023-06-14T12:51:29.654819Z",
		"resourceOwner": "163840776835432705"
	},
	"authUrl": "https://accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/v2/auth?..."
}
```

--------------------------------

### Create Session for Passkey Login

Source: https://zitadel.com/docs/guides/integrate/login-ui/passkey

Initiate a session with ZITADEL to receive the necessary WebAuthN challenge options.

```bash
curl --request POST \
  --url https://${CUSTOM_DOMAIN}/v2/sessions \
  --header 'Accept: application/json' \
  --header 'Authorization: Bearer '"$TOKEN"''\
  --header 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  --data '{
  "checks": {
    "user": {
      "loginName": "minni-mouse@mouse.com"
    }
  },
  "metadata": {},
  "challenges": {
    "webAuthN": {
      "domain": "example.domain.com",
      "userVerificationRequirement": "USER_VERIFICATION_REQUIREMENT_REQUIRED"
    }
  }
}'
```

```json
{
  "details": {
    "sequence": "2",
    "changeDate": "2023-06-27",
    "resourceOwner": "69629023906488334"
  },
  "sessionId": "d654e6ba-70a3-48ef-a95d-37c8d8a7901a",
  "sessionToken": "string",
  "challenges": {
    "webAuthN": {
      "publicKeyCredentialRequestOptions": {
        "publicKey": {
          "allowCredentials": [
            {
              "id": "REDACTED",
              "type": "public-key"
            }
          ],
          "challenge": "REDACTED",
          "rpId": "example.domain.com",
          "timeout": 300000,
          "userVerification": "required"
        }
      }
    }
  }
}

### API Authentication Example

Source: https://github.com/zitadel/zitadel-go/blob/main/README.md

Shows how to secure a REST API with Zitadel tokens, including public, token-protected, and role-based endpoints.

```go
// This example shows how to secure a REST API, where different endpoints require a valid ZITADEL token for access.
// It includes:
// - A public endpoint accessible without a token.
// - A private endpoint accessible with any valid token.
// - An administrator endpoint accessible only by users with a specific role.
// See the ./example/api directory for the full implementation.
```

### Private Key JWT Authentication with Zitadel Go Client

Source: https://github.com/zitadel/zitadel-go/blob/main/README.md

Demonstrates how to authenticate with the Zitadel API using a private key stored in a JSON file. This method is recommended for production due to its strong security. It involves building the client with a service user authentication option that uses the private key to sign JWTs.

```go
package main

import (
	"context"
	"log"
	"os"
	"log/slog"

	"github.com/zitadel/oidc/v3/pkg/oidc"

	"github.com/zitadel/zitadel-go/v3/pkg/client"
	"github.com/zitadel/zitadel-go/v3/pkg/client/zitadel/management"
	"github.com/zitadel/zitadel-go/v3/pkg/zitadel"
)

func main() {
	domain := "https://example.us1.zitadel.cloud"
	keyPath := "path/to/jwt-key.json"

	ctx := context.Background()

	authOption := client.DefaultServiceUserAuthentication(
		keyPath,
		oidc.ScopeOpenID,
		client.ScopeZitadelAPI(),
	)

	api, err := client.New(ctx, zitadel.New(domain), client.WithAuth(authOption))
	if err != nil {
		slog.Error("could not create api client", "error", err)
		os.Exit(1)
	}

	resp, err := api.ManagementService().GetMyOrg(ctx, &management.GetMyOrgRequest{})
	if err != nil {
		slog.Error("gRPC call failed", "error", err)
		os.Exit(1)
	}

	log.Printf("Successfully called API: Your organization is %s", resp.GetOrg().GetName())
}
```

### Personal Access Token (PAT) Authentication with Zitadel Go Client

Source: https://github.com/zitadel/zitadel-go/blob/main/README.md

Shows how to authenticate using a Personal Access Token (PAT). This method is convenient for development and testing as it avoids the need for dynamic token generation. The client is initialized with the `PAT` authentication option.

```go
package main

import (
	"context"
	"log"
	"os"
	"log/slog"

	"github.com/zitadel/zitadel-go/v3/pkg/client"
	"github.com/zitadel/zitadel-go/v3/pkg/client/zitadel/management"
	"github.com/zitadel/zitadel-go/v3/pkg/zitadel"
)

func main() {
	domain := "https://example.us1.zitadel.cloud"
	token := "token"

	ctx := context.Background()

	authOption := client.PAT(token)

	api, err := client.New(ctx, zitadel.New(domain), client.WithAuth(authOption))
	if err != nil {
		slog.Error("could not create api client", "error", err)
		os.Exit(1)
	}

	resp, err := api.ManagementService().GetMyOrg(ctx, &management.GetMyOrgRequest{})
	if err != nil {
		slog.Error("gRPC call failed", "error", err)
		os.Exit(1)
	}

	log.Printf("Successfully called API: Your organization is %s", resp.GetOrg().GetName())
}
```

### Client Credentials Grant with Zitadel Go Client

Source: https://github.com/zitadel/zitadel-go/blob/main/README.md

Illustrates how to authenticate using the Client Credentials Grant, which relies on a client ID and client secret. This method is suitable for simple, server-to-server communication in trusted environments. The client is built using the `PasswordAuthentication` option.

```go
package main

import (
	"context"
	"log"
	"os"
	"log/slog"

	"github.com/zitadel/oidc/v3/pkg/oidc"

	"github.com/zitadel/zitadel-go/v3/pkg/client"
	"github.com/zitadel/zitadel-go/v3/pkg/client/zitadel/management"
	"github.com/zitadel/zitadel-go/v3/pkg/zitadel"
)

func main() {
	domain := "https://example.us1.zitadel.cloud"
	clientID := "id"
	clientSecret := "secret"

	ctx := context.Background()

	authOption := client.PasswordAuthentication(
		clientID,
		clientSecret,
		oidc.ScopeOpenID,
		client.ScopeZitadelAPI(),
	)

	api, err := client.New(ctx, zitadel.New(domain), client.WithAuth(authOption))
	if err != nil {
		slog.Error("could not create api client", "error", err)
		os.Exit(1)
	}

	resp, err := api.ManagementService().GetMyOrg(ctx, &management.GetMyOrgRequest{})
	if err != nil {
		slog.Error("gRPC call failed", "error", err)
		os.Exit(1)
	}

	log.Printf("Successfully called API: Your organization is %s", resp.GetOrg().GetName())
}
```
```

Good research done. Next question:

**Zitadel's multi-tenancy model** — Zitadel has built-in organizations (like Keycloak realms). For Raven's multi-tenant isolation, which approach?

- **A) One Zitadel organization per Raven tenant** — each customer gets their own Zitadel org. Zitadel manages org creation via API. Maps cleanly to your existing Keycloak realm-per-tenant model.
- **B) Single Zitadel organization, Raven manages tenancy** — all users in one Zitadel org, Raven's Go backend handles tenant isolation via its own org/workspace model + PostgreSQL RLS. Simpler Zitadel config, your app owns the multi-tenancy.

I'd recommend **B** — Zitadel handles identity (who are you?), Raven handles tenancy (what org/workspace do you belong to?). This is simpler to operate, avoids Zitadel API calls for every org creation, and your RLS model already works. Zitadel just needs to issue JWTs with the user ID — Raven looks up org membership in its own database.

> DEVELOPER

B

> AGENT

Good — Zitadel for identity, Raven for tenancy. Simpler architecture.

Next question: **What should the user registration flow look like?**

- **A) Landing page → Google login only** — "Get Started Free" redirects straight to Google OAuth (via Zitadel). After Google auth, Raven auto-creates the user + org. No email/password option at all.
- **B) Landing page → choice screen** — small page with "Continue with Google" button + "Sign in with Passkey" option. No email/password. After auth, Raven auto-provisions.
- **C) Landing page → Google first, passkey later** — first signup is always Google (simplest onboarding). After account exists, user can register a passkey in settings for future logins. Return visits show both options.

I'd recommend **C** — it eliminates choice paralysis on first signup (one button: "Continue with Google"), gets users in fastest, and passkeys become a power-user feature they add after they're invested. WebAuthn registration requires an existing account anyway.

- **A) Full auto-provision** — after Google login, Raven silently creates: user record, default org (named after user's Google name), default workspace, free tier billing assignment. User lands directly on the dashboard. No wizard.
- **B) Minimal wizard (2 steps)** — after Google login, quick wizard: Step 1: "Name your organization" (pre-filled from Google name), Step 2: "Create your first knowledge base" (name + optional file upload). Then dashboard.
- **C) Existing 5-step wizard** — keep the current onboarding wizard (org name → workspace → knowledge base → test chat → done) but wire it to real APIs.

**What's the deployment target for this sub-project?** The landing page lives at `raven.ravencloak.org`. Where does the app + Zitadel go?

- **A) Same EC2 instance** — Zitadel + Raven API + frontend all on your existing `ec2-65-0-85-115.ap-south-1.compute.amazonaws.com`, behind Traefik with subdomains: `auth.ravencloak.org` (Zitadel), `app.ravencloak.org` (frontend), `api.ravencloak.org` (Go API)

OK, I have everything I need. Here are **3 approaches** for the Keycloak → Zitadel migration:

---

### Approach 1: "Big Bang Swap"
Replace Keycloak with Zitadel in one shot across all layers (backend, frontend, Docker Compose, deploy).

**Pros**: Clean cut, no dual-auth complexity, one PR
**Cons**: Large blast radius, hard to test incrementally, if anything breaks it's hard to isolate

### Approach 2: "Backend First, Frontend Second" (Recommended)
Three phases in separate PRs:

1. **Infrastructure** — Add Zitadel to Docker Compose, remove Keycloak, configure CF tunnels, write Zitadel init config (Google IDP, OIDC app)
2. **Backend** — Replace Keycloak JWT middleware with Zitadel OIDC validation (`zitadel/oidc` Go library), update user model to use Zitadel user IDs, add post-signup org auto-creation endpoint
3. **Frontend** — Replace `keycloak-js` with standard OIDC client (Zitadel-compatible), build the 2-step onboarding wizard, add passkey registration in settings

**Pros**: Each phase is testable independently, smaller PRs, backend can be verified with curl before frontend touches it
**Cons**: 3 PRs instead of 1, brief period where nothing works end-to-end between phases

### Approach 3: "Adapter Pattern"
Build an auth abstraction layer in Go that supports both Keycloak and Zitadel, then swap the implementation behind the interface.

**Pros**: Zero-downtime migration, can A/B test
**Cons**: Over-engineered for a project with no production users yet, adds unnecessary abstraction

---

**My recommendation: Approach 2** — you have no production users to migrate, so there's no need for an adapter layer. But the work is complex enough that doing it in 3 testable phases prevents a debugging nightmare. Each PR can be verified before moving to the next.

Now let me present the design section by section.

**Section 1: Infrastructure — Zitadel + Cloudflare Tunnels**

**Docker Compose changes:**
- Remove: `keycloak` service, `keycloak-postgres` (if separate)
- Add: `zitadel` service (`ghcr.io/zitadel/zitadel:latest`) using Raven's existing PostgreSQL with a separate `zitadel` database
- Add: `cloudflared` service for tunnel routing
- Zitadel runs on internal port `8080`, exposed only via CF tunnel as `auth.ravencloak.org`

**Zitadel init config** (mounted YAML files):
- `zitadel-config.yaml` — database connection (shared PG instance, `zitadel` database), logging, external domain `auth.ravencloak.org`, TLS disabled (CF tunnel handles it)
- `zitadel-init-steps.yaml` — auto-creates on first boot:
  - Default OIDC application (`raven-app`) with redirect URIs for `app.ravencloak.org` and `localhost:5173`
  - Google IDP configured (client ID/secret from env vars)
  - Default redirect behavior: skip Zitadel login page, go straight to Google

**Cloudflare Tunnel config:**
- Single `cloudflared` container with tunnel token from env var
- Routes: `auth.ravencloak.org → zitadel:8080`, `api.ravencloak.org → api:8081`, `app.ravencloak.org → frontend:3000`
- Local dev: no `cloudflared`, services accessed directly on `localhost` ports

**Environment variables** (`.env`):
- `ZITADEL_MASTERKEY` — 32-char random encryption key
- `ZITADEL_EXTERNALDOMAIN` — `auth.ravencloak.org` (prod) or `localhost` (dev)
- `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`, `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` — for Google IDP
- `CF_TUNNEL_TOKEN` — Cloudflare tunnel token (prod only)

Does this infrastructure section look right?

> DEVELOPER

yes

> AGENT

**Section 2: Backend — Zitadel OIDC Middleware + Post-Signup Flow**

**JWT Middleware replacement:**
- Remove: Keycloak-specific JWT validation (`internal/middleware/auth.go`)
- Add: Zitadel OIDC token verification using `github.com/zitadel/oidc/v3` library
- The middleware introspects the JWT, extracts `sub` (Zitadel user ID) and `email` claims
- No more realm-based routing — single Zitadel issuer, Raven handles tenant lookup internally

**User model changes:**
- `users` table: `keycloak_id` column → rename to `external_id` (stores Zitadel user ID)
- Add `auth_provider` column (`zitadel`, `google`, etc.) for future flexibility
- Lookup: on every authenticated request, middleware resolves `external_id` → internal `user_id` → `org_id` via the existing `user_orgs` table

**Post-signup auto-provisioning endpoint:**
- `POST /api/v1/auth/callback` — called after first Google login
- Flow:
  1. Validate Zitadel JWT
  2. Check if `external_id` exists in `users` table
  3. If **new user**: create user record, return `{ isNewUser: true }`
  4. If **existing user**: return `{ isNewUser: false, orgId: ... }`
- Frontend uses `isNewUser` flag to route to onboarding wizard or dashboard

**Org creation endpoint (used by onboarding wizard step 1):**
- `POST /api/v1/orgs` — no longer requires `org_admin` role pre-assigned
- If user has no org yet (checked via `user_orgs` table), allow creation
- Creates: org record, `user_orgs` entry with `owner` role, free tier billing assignment
- Returns: `orgId`, `orgSlug`

**Workspace + KB creation (onboarding wizard step 2):**
- `POST /api/v1/workspaces` — creates default workspace in the new org
- `POST /api/v1/knowledge-bases` — creates first KB in the workspace
- Both already exist in the API, just need the auth middleware swap to work with Zitadel tokens

Does this backend section look right?

> DEVELOPER

yes

> AGENT

**Section 3: Frontend — OIDC Client + 2-Step Onboarding**

**Auth library swap:**
- Remove: `keycloak-js` dependency and all Keycloak-specific code in `frontend/src/`
- Add: `oidc-client-ts` (standard OIDC library, works with any provider including Zitadel)
- Auth flow: Authorization Code + PKCE (same as Keycloak was using, just different issuer URL)
- Config: issuer `https://auth.ravencloak.org`, client ID from env var, redirect URI `https://app.ravencloak.org/callback`

**Login flow (user perspective):**
1. User clicks "Get Started Free" on landing page → `app.ravencloak.org/login`
2. `/login` page immediately redirects to Zitadel auth endpoint with `idp_hint=google` parameter (skips Zitadel login UI, goes straight to Google)
3. Google auth → redirect back to `app.ravencloak.org/callback`
4. Callback page: exchanges code for tokens, calls `POST /api/v1/auth/callback`
5. If `isNewUser: true` → redirect to `/onboarding`
6. If `isNewUser: false` → redirect to `/dashboard`

**2-step onboarding wizard:**
- Replace the existing 5-step wizard with 2 focused steps
- **Step 1: "Name your organization"**
  - Text input pre-filled with user's Google display name + "'s Team"
  - "Continue" button → calls `POST /api/v1/orgs`
- **Step 2: "Create your first knowledge base"**
  - KB name input (required)
  - Optional file upload zone (drag & drop PDF/DOCX)
  - "Get Started" button → calls `POST /api/v1/workspaces` then `POST /api/v1/knowledge-bases`, optionally triggers ingestion
  - Redirects to dashboard

**Passkey registration (settings page, not onboarding):**
- Add to existing settings/security page: "Add Passkey" button
- Uses Zitadel's WebAuthn API: `POST /v2/users/{userId}/passkeys` to get creation options
- Calls `navigator.credentials.create()` with the options
- Sends result back to Zitadel to register the passkey
- Future logins: if passkey exists, show "Sign in with Passkey" option alongside Google on the login page

**Router guards:**
- Unauthenticated → redirect to `/login`
- Authenticated + no org → redirect to `/onboarding`
- Authenticated + has org → allow dashboard access

Does this frontend section look right?

{
  "file_path": "REDACTED.md",
  "content": "# Zitadel Migration — Design Spec\n\n**Date**: 2026-04-13\n**Status**: Approved\n**Sub-project**: 1 of 3 (Zitadel migration → Google IDP + WebAuthn → Registration + Org flow)\n\n---\n\n## Overview\n\nReplace Keycloak with Zitadel as the identity provider for Raven. Zitadel handles identity (authentication, Google IDP, passkeys). Raven handles tenancy (org/workspace membership, RLS, billing). This is a full replacement — no dual-auth period.\n\nThe migration is delivered in **3 phases** (separate PRs):\n1. Infrastructure — Docker Compose + Zitadel config + Cloudflare Tunnels\n2. Backend — Go middleware swap + post-signup provisioning endpoints\n3. Frontend — OIDC client swap + 2-step onboarding wizard + passkey registration\n\n## Architecture Decisions\n\n| Decision | Choice | Rationale |\n|----------|--------|-----------|\n| Multi-tenancy | Single Zitadel org, Raven manages tenancy | Simpler ops, existing RLS model works, no Zitadel API calls for org creation |\n| First signup | Google OAuth only | Eliminates choice paralysis, fastest onboarding, passkeys added later |\n| Passkey timing | Post-registration in settings | WebAuthn requires existing account, power-user feature |\n| Onboarding | 2-step wizard (name org → create KB) | Minimal friction, gets user invested fast |\n| Deployment | Docker Compose + Cloudflare Tunnels | Same config local and prod, no TLS management, no exposed ports |\n| Reverse proxy | Cloudflare Tunnels (replaces Traefik) | Zero-config TLS, DDoS protection, no port exposure |\n\n## Domain Map\n\n| Domain | Service | Internal Port |\n|--------|---------|---------------|\n| `auth.ravencloak.org` | Zitadel | 8080 |\n| `api.ravencloak.org` | Raven Go API | 8081 |\n| `app.ravencloak.org` | Vue.js frontend | 3000 |\n| `raven.ravencloak.org` | Landing page | Static (Cloudflare Pages) |\n\n---\n\n## Phase 1: Infrastructure\n\n### Docker Compose Changes\n\n**Remove:**\n- `keycloak` service\n- Keycloak-specific PostgreSQL database/config\n- `deploy/keycloak/` directory (realm config, themes)\n- Traefik service and config (if present)\n\n**Add:**\n\n```yaml\nzitadel:\n  image: ghcr.io/zitadel/zitadel:latest\n  restart: always\n  command: >\n    start-from-init\n    --config /zitadel-config.yaml\n    --config /zitadel-secrets.yaml\n    --steps /zitadel-init-steps.yaml\n    --masterkey \"${ZITADEL_MASTERKEY}\"\n    --tlsMode disabled\n  ports:\n    - \"8080:8080\"\n  volumes:\n    - ./deploy/zitadel/zitadel-config.yaml:/zitadel-config.yaml:ro\n    - ./deploy/zitadel/zitadel-secrets.yaml:/zitadel-secrets.yaml:ro\n    - ./deploy/zitadel/zitadel-init-steps.yaml:/zitadel-init-steps.yaml:ro\n  depends_on:\n    postgres:\n      condition: service_healthy\n  environment:\n    - ZITADEL_MASTERKEY=${ZITADEL_MASTERKEY}\n\ncloudflared:\n  image: cloudflare/cloudflared:latest\n  restart: always\n  command: tunnel run\n  environment:\n    - TUNNEL_TOKEN=${CF_TUNNEL_TOKEN}\n  profiles:\n    - production\n```\n\n**Zitadel uses Raven's existing PostgreSQL** — separate `zitadel` database on the same instance. No additional PG container needed.\n\n**`cloudflared` uses `profiles: [production]`** — only starts when running `docker compose --profile production up`. Local dev accesses services directly on localhost ports.\n\n### Zitadel Configuration Files\n\n**`deploy/zitadel/zitadel-config.yaml`:**\n```yaml\nLog:\n  Level: info\n\nExternalDomain: ${ZITADEL_EXTERNALDOMAIN}\nExternalSecure: true\nExternalPort: 443\n\nDatabase:\n  postgres:\n    Host: postgres\n    Port: 5432\n    Database: zitadel\n    User:\n      Username: zitadel\n      Password: ${ZITADEL_DB_PASSWORD}\n    Admin:\n      Username: postgres\n      Password: ${POSTGRES_PASSWORD}\n    MaxOpenConns: 10\n    MaxIdleConns: 5\n```\n\n**`deploy/zitadel/zitadel-init-steps.yaml`:**\nAuto-creates on first boot:\n- Default OIDC application `raven-app` with:\n  - Redirect URIs: `https://app.ravencloak.org/callback`, `http://localhost:5173/callback`\n  - Post-logout URIs: `https://app.ravencloak.org`, `http://localhost:5173`\n  - Grant type: Authorization Code + PKCE\n  - Response type: Code\n- Machine user `raven-api` with key (for backend-to-Zitadel API calls if needed)\n\n**`deploy/zitadel/zitadel-secrets.yaml`:**\n```yaml\nDatabase:\n  postgres:\n    User:\n      Password: ${ZITADEL_DB_PASSWORD}\n    Admin:\n      Password: ${POSTGRES_PASSWORD}\n```\n\n### Google IDP Setup\n\nConfigured via Zitadel admin console (post-init) or API:\n- Add Google as generic OIDC IDP\n- Client ID/Secret from environment: `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`, `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`\n- Scopes: `openid`, `profile`, `email`\n- Set as default IDP for the organization\n- Configure auto-linking: match by email\n\n### Cloudflare Tunnel Routes\n\nSingle tunnel with three public hostname routes:\n\n| Public Hostname | Service | Internal URL |\n|-----------------|---------|-------------|\n| `auth.ravencloak.org` | Zitadel | `http://zitadel:8080` |\n| `api.ravencloak.org` | Raven API | `http://api:8081` |\n| `app.ravencloak.org` | Frontend | `http://frontend:3000` |\n\nConfigured in Cloudflare Zero Trust dashboard. The `cloudflared` container just needs `CF_TUNNEL_TOKEN`.\n\n### Environment Variables\n\n```env\n# Zitadel\nZITADEL_MASTERKEY=<32-char-random-key>\nZITADEL_EXTERNALDOMAIN=auth.ravencloak.org  # or localhost for dev\nZITADEL_DB_PASSWORD=<password>\n\n# Google IDP\nGOOGLE_CLIENT_ID=<from-google-cloud-console>\nGOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET=<from-google-cloud-console>\n\n# Cloudflare (production only)\nCF_TUNNEL_TOKEN=<from-cloudflare-dashboard>\n\n# Existing\nPOSTGRES_PASSWORD=<existing-pg-password>\n```\n\n### Local Development\n\n- No `cloudflared` (uses `profiles: [production]`)\n- Zitadel accessible at `http://localhost:8080`\n- Frontend at `http://localhost:5173` (Vite dev server)\n- API at `http://localhost:8081`\n- Zitadel config uses `ExternalDomain: localhost`, `ExternalSecure: false`, `ExternalPort: 8080` (overridden via env or local config file)\n\n---\n\n## Phase 2: Backend\n\n### JWT Middleware Replacement\n\n**Remove:**\n- `internal/middleware/auth.go` — Keycloak-specific JWT validation\n- Keycloak realm-based routing logic\n- `keycloak` Go dependencies\n\n**Add:**\n- New `internal/middleware/auth.go` using `github.com/zitadel/oidc/v3/pkg/op` for token introspection\n- Or use standard JWT validation with Zitadel's JWKS endpoint: `https://auth.ravencloak.org/.well-known/openid-configuration`\n- Middleware extracts from JWT: `sub` (Zitadel user ID), `email`, `name`\n- Sets on Gin context: `userExternalID`, `userEmail`, `userName`\n\n**Token validation flow:**\n1. Extract `Authorization: Bearer <token>` from request header\n2. Validate JWT signature against Zitadel JWKS (cached)\n3. Verify `iss` matches Zitadel domain, `aud` matches `raven-app` client ID\n4. Extract claims, set on context\n5. Resolve `external_id` → internal `user_id` + `org_id` via database lookup\n\n### User Model Changes\n\n**Migration:**\n```sql\nALTER TABLE users RENAME COLUMN keycloak_id TO external_id;\nALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN auth_provider TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'zitadel';\nCREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_users_external_id ON users(external_id);\n```\n\n**Lookup path:**\n- Request → middleware extracts `sub` from JWT → query `users WHERE external_id = sub` → get `user_id` → query `user_orgs WHERE user_id = ?` → get `org_id`\n- Cache this lookup in Valkey with TTL (avoid DB hit on every request)\n\n### New/Modified Endpoints\n\n**`POST /api/v1/auth/callback`** (new, authenticated):\n- Called by frontend after OIDC callback\n- Checks if `external_id` exists in `users` table\n- If new user:\n  - Creates `users` record (external_id, email, name from JWT claims)\n  - Returns `{ isNewUser: true }`\n- If existing user:\n  - Returns `{ isNewUser: false, orgId: \"...\", orgSlug: \"...\" }`\n\n**`POST /api/v1/orgs`** (modified):\n- Remove requirement for `org_admin` role pre-assignment\n- New rule: if authenticated user has zero orgs (via `user_orgs`), allow org creation\n- Creates: org record, `user_orgs` entry with `owner` role, free tier subscription via billing service\n- Returns: `{ orgId, orgSlug }`\n\n**Existing endpoints (unchanged, just work with new middleware):**\n- `POST /api/v1/workspaces` — creates workspace in user's org\n- `POST /api/v1/knowledge-bases` — creates KB in workspace\n- `POST /api/v1/documents` — upload documents to KB\n\n### Keycloak Cleanup\n\nRemove from Go codebase:\n- Keycloak admin client usage (realm provisioning in `internal/service/provision.go`)\n- Keycloak-specific config in `internal/config/config.go`\n- Keycloak health check endpoints\n\nAdd to Go config:\n```go\ntype ZitadelConfig struct {\n    Domain   string `env:\"ZITADEL_EXTERNALDOMAIN\" envDefault:\"localhost:8080\"`\n    ClientID string `env:\"ZITADEL_CLIENT_ID\"`\n    Secure   bool   `env:\"ZITADEL_SECURE\" envDefault:\"true\"`\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Phase 3: Frontend\n\n### Auth Library Swap\n\n**Remove:**\n- `keycloak-js` dependency\n- `frontend/src/auth/keycloak.ts` (or equivalent)\n- All `keycloak.init()`, `keycloak.token`, `keycloak.login()` references\n\n**Add:**\n- `oidc-client-ts` dependency (standard OIDC Relying Party library)\n- `frontend/src/auth/oidc.ts` — OIDC client configuration:\n  ```typescript\n  const config = {\n    authority: import.meta.env.VITE_ZITADEL_URL, // https://auth.ravencloak.org\n    client_id: import.meta.env.VITE_ZITADEL_CLIENT_ID,\n    redirect_uri: `${window.location.origin}/callback`,\n    post_logout_redirect_uri: window.location.origin,\n    scope: 'openid profile email',\n    response_type: 'code',\n  }\n  ```\n\n### Login Flow\n\n**`/login` route:**\n- On mount: immediately trigger OIDC redirect with `idp_hint=google` extra param\n- This makes Zitadel skip its own login UI and redirect straight to Google\n- User sees: Landing page → Google consent → app callback (no Zitadel UI ever shown)\n\n**`/callback` route:**\n- Exchange authorization code for tokens (PKCE)\n- Store tokens in memory (not localStorage — security)\n- Call `POST /api/v1/auth/callback` with the access token\n- If `isNewUser: true` → navigate to `/onboarding`\n- If `isNewUser: false` → navigate to `/dashboard`\n\n**`/logout`:**\n- Clear tokens from memory\n- Redirect to Zitadel end-session endpoint\n- Zitadel redirects back to landing page\n\n### 2-Step Onboarding Wizard\n\n**Replaces** the existing 5-step wizard at `frontend/src/pages/onboarding/`.\n\n**Step 1: \"Name your organization\"**\n- Full-screen centered card\n- Text input pre-filled with `${userName}'s Team`\n- Validation: 3-100 chars, alphanumeric + spaces\n- \"Continue\" button → `POST /api/v1/orgs` with `{ name: input }`\n- On success: store `orgId` in state, advance to step 2\n\n**Step 2: \"Create your first knowledge base\"**\n- KB name input (required, e.g., \"Product Docs\")\n- Drag-and-drop file upload zone (optional, accepts PDF/DOCX/MD)\n- \"Get Started\" button →\n  1. `POST /api/v1/workspaces` → creates default workspace\n  2. `POST /api/v1/knowledge-bases` → creates KB\n  3. If files uploaded: `POST /api/v1/documents` for each file\n  4. Navigate to `/dashboard`\n\n**Styling**: follows existing frontend patterns (Vue 3 + Tailwind), black/white/amber accent consistent with landing page.\n\n### Passkey Registration (Settings Page)\n\nAdded to existing settings/security page (not part of onboarding):\n\n**\"Add Passkey\" button:**\n1. Calls Zitadel API: `POST /v2/users/{userId}/passkeys` to get `publicKeyCredentialCreationOptions`\n2. Calls `navigator.credentials.create({ publicKey: options })` in browser\n3. Sends credential response back to Zitadel: `POST /v2/users/{userId}/passkeys/{passkeyId}`\n4. Shows success: \"Passkey registered. You can now sign in without Google.\"\n\n**Login page update (after passkey exists):**\n- Check if user has passkeys via Zitadel user info\n- If yes: show two buttons — \"Continue with Google\" + \"Sign in with Passkey\"\n- If no: show only \"Continue with Google\"\n- Passkey login: create Zitadel session with WebAuthn challenge, verify in browser, complete session\n\n### Router Guards\n\n```typescript\nrouter.beforeEach((to) => {\n  const auth = useAuthStore()\n\n  if (to.meta.requiresAuth && !auth.isAuthenticated) {\n    return '/login'\n  }\n\n  if (auth.isAuthenticated && !auth.hasOrg && to.path !== '/onboarding') {\n    return '/onboarding'\n  }\n})\n```\n\n### Environment Variables (Frontend)\n\n```env\nVITE_ZITADEL_URL=https://auth.ravencloak.org\nVITE_ZITADEL_CLIENT_ID=<from-zitadel-init>\nVITE_API_URL=https://api.ravencloak.org\n```\n\n---\n\n## Files Changed Summary\n\n### Phase 1 (Infrastructure)\n- Remove: `deploy/keycloak/` directory\n- Create: `deploy/zitadel/zitadel-config.yaml`\n- Create: `deploy/zitadel/zitadel-secrets.yaml`\n- Create: `deploy/zitadel/zitadel-init-steps.yaml`\n- Modify: `docker-compose.yml` (remove keycloak, add zitadel + cloudflared)\n- Modify: `.env.example` (new Zitadel + CF vars)\n\n### Phase 2 (Backend)\n- Create: DB migration (rename keycloak_id → external_id, add auth_provider)\n- Modify: `internal/middleware/auth.go` (Zitadel JWT validation)\n- Modify: `internal/config/config.go` (ZitadelConfig replaces KeycloakConfig)\n- Modify: `internal/handler/auth.go` (new callback endpoint)\n- Modify: `internal/handler/provision.go` (remove Keycloak realm provisioning)\n- Modify: `internal/service/provision.go` (remove Keycloak admin client)\n- Modify: `internal/handler/routing.go` (new route for auth callback)\n- Modify: `go.mod` (remove keycloak deps, add zitadel/oidc)\n\n### Phase 3 (Frontend)\n- Remove: `keycloak-js` dependency\n- Add: `oidc-client-ts` dependency\n- Create: `frontend/src/auth/oidc.ts` (OIDC client config)\n- Modify: `frontend/src/auth/` (replace Keycloak with OIDC)\n- Modify: `frontend/src/pages/onboarding/` (2-step wizard replaces 5-step)\n- Modify: `frontend/src/pages/settings/` (add passkey registration)\n- Modify: `frontend/src/router/` (update guards, add callback route)\n- Modify: `frontend/.env.example` (Zitadel vars replace Keycloak vars)\n- Modify: `frontend/package.json` (swap deps)\n\n---\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Voice/WhatsApp connector setup per org (separate sub-project)\n- User invitation / team member management\n- SSO/SAML for enterprise orgs (future, Zitadel supports it natively)\n- Migrating existing Keycloak users (no production users exist)\n- Custom Zitadel branding/themes (not needed — users never see Zitadel UI)\n"
}
