Now I have a thorough understanding of the codebase and the spec. Here is my review.

---

## Design Spec Review: Zitadel Migration

**Spec**: `REDACTED.md`

### What the spec does well

The spec is well-structured across 3 clean phases with clear remove/add/modify lists per phase. The architecture decision to keep Zitadel as identity-only while Raven manages tenancy via PostgreSQL RLS is sound and avoids coupling multi-tenancy to the IDP. The domain map, environment variable inventory, and Cloudflare Tunnels approach are all practical. The "Out of Scope" section is explicit and appropriate.

---

### MUST-FIX (Blocking)

**1. Wrong column name in migration -- spec says `keycloak_id` but codebase uses `keycloak_sub`**

The spec's migration (line 210) says:
```sql
ALTER TABLE users RENAME COLUMN keycloak_id TO external_id;
```

But the actual column is `keycloak_sub` (see `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/migrations/00004_users.sql` line 7). The Go model field is `KeycloakSub` (`/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/model/user.go` line 21), the repository queries reference `keycloak_sub` (`/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/repository/user.go` line 23), and the index is `idx_users_keycloak_sub`. The spec must use the correct column name or the migration will fail.

**2. Missing migration for `organizations.keycloak_realm` column**

The `organizations` table has a `keycloak_realm` column (`/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/migrations/00003_organizations.sql` line 8) and the Go model has `KeycloakRealm` (`/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/model/org.go` line 22). The org repository scans it (`/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/repository/org.go` line 24). The spec only addresses the `users` table migration but never mentions dropping or renaming `organizations.keycloak_realm`. This column should be dropped or repurposed in the migration, and the Go model/repository updated.

**3. The `users` table is scoped to `org_id` -- spec's new user flow is incompatible**

The current `users` table requires `org_id NOT NULL` (`/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/migrations/00004_users.sql` line 4: `org_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES organizations(id)`). But the spec's `POST /api/v1/auth/callback` flow creates a user record *before* org creation (Phase 2, line 226-228: "Creates users record (external_id, email, name from JWT claims)"). This will fail the NOT NULL constraint since the user has no org yet.

The spec needs to either: (a) make `org_id` nullable on `users` and handle the interim state, (b) introduce a separate `user_accounts` table that is org-independent, or (c) defer user record creation until after org creation in onboarding and track the authenticated-but-unprovisioned state differently (e.g., in the JWT/session only).

**4. No `user_orgs` table exists -- spec references it but it does not exist**

The spec (Phase 2, line 217) says: "query `user_orgs WHERE user_id = ?` -> get `org_id`". But there is no `user_orgs` table anywhere in the codebase or migrations. Currently, org membership is determined by the `users.org_id` column directly (one user belongs to one org). The spec needs to either create this table in its migration or use the existing `users.org_id` column for the lookup path.

**5. `POST /api/v1/orgs` currently requires `org_admin` role -- spec describes removing this but is vague**

In `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/cmd/api/main.go` line 444:
```go
api.POST("/orgs", middleware.RequireOrgRole("org_admin"), orgHandler.Create)
```

The spec says "Remove requirement for org_admin role pre-assignment" and replace with "if authenticated user has zero orgs, allow org creation". This is correct conceptually but the implementation detail is missing: the current JWT middleware extracts `org_id` from JWT claims (Keycloak puts it there). With Zitadel, the new middleware only gets `sub`, `email`, `name`. The spec needs to describe how the middleware resolves org context for authenticated users who have no org yet (the whole `ContextKeyOrgID` flow in auth.go). Currently, every billing/workspace/KB endpoint reads `ContextKeyOrgID` from the Gin context -- a user with no org would get empty string everywhere.

**6. Shared PostgreSQL -- missing `CREATE DATABASE zitadel` and Zitadel DB user in init.sql**

The spec says "Zitadel uses Raven's existing PostgreSQL -- separate `zitadel` database on the same instance" but never specifies creating the `zitadel` database or the `zitadel` PostgreSQL user. The current `deploy/postgres/init.sql` creates the `keycloak` database. The spec needs to add `CREATE DATABASE zitadel` and `CREATE USER zitadel WITH PASSWORD ...` to `init.sql` (or document that Zitadel's `start-from-init` handles this via the Admin credentials -- which it does, but only if the Admin user has `CREATEDB` privilege, which the default `raven` PG user may not have).

**7. Port conflict: go-api and Zitadel both claim port 8080**

The current `docker-compose.yml` maps `go-api` to port 8080 (`ports: - "8080:8080"`). The spec's Zitadel service also uses port 8080 (`ports: - "8080:8080"`). The spec says API should be on 8081 in the domain map, but never specifies changing the go-api port in `docker-compose.yml` or in `internal/config/config.go` (where the default is `v.SetDefault("server.port", 8080)` at line 227).

**8. `zitadel-config.yaml` uses environment variable interpolation -- Zitadel does not support this**

The spec shows `${ZITADEL_EXTERNALDOMAIN}` and `${ZITADEL_DB_PASSWORD}` inside `zitadel-config.yaml`. Zitadel's YAML config files do not support shell-style variable substitution. You need either: (a) use Docker Compose environment variables and `--config` overrides via command flags, (b) use `envsubst` in an entrypoint wrapper, or (c) use Zitadel's environment variable override format (`ZITADEL_EXTERNALDOMAIN` as an env var directly -- Zitadel maps env vars to config keys automatically). The spec should clarify this mechanism.

---

### NICE-TO-HAVE (Non-blocking improvements)

**1. Zitadel image tag should be pinned, not `latest`**

The spec uses `ghcr.io/zitadel/zitadel:latest`. Every other service in docker-compose.yml uses versioned tags (e.g., `pgvector/pgvector:pg18`, `valkey/valkey:8.1-alpine`, `quay.io/keycloak/keycloak:26`). Pin to a specific version like `ghcr.io/zitadel/zitadel:v2.71.0` for reproducible builds.

**2. Google IDP setup is manual ("configured via Zitadel admin console (post-init) or API") -- should be automated**

The spec says Google IDP is configured via admin console post-init. This breaks the "same config local and prod" goal stated in the Architecture Decisions. Consider using Zitadel's management API via the `raven-api` machine user (already planned in init-steps) to automate this in a startup script or init container. Otherwise, every fresh deployment requires manual IDP configuration.

**3. Missing `idp_hint` parameter name for Zitadel**

The spec says to use `idp_hint=google` to skip Zitadel's login UI. Zitadel's actual parameter for this is the IDP's Zitadel-internal ID (a numeric/UUID value), not the string `"google"`. The spec should note that the `idp_hint` value needs to be the Zitadel IDP ID obtained after Google IDP registration, and this value should be stored as a frontend environment variable.

**4. Passkey registration calls Zitadel API directly from frontend -- CORS and auth concerns**

Phase 3 describes the frontend calling `POST /v2/users/{userId}/passkeys` directly on the Zitadel API. This requires: (a) Zitadel CORS to allow `app.ravencloak.org`, (b) the user's Zitadel access token to have sufficient scopes. The spec should either proxy these calls through the Go API backend or document the required Zitadel CORS and scope configuration.

**5. Keycloak webhook handler and SPI model not listed for removal**

The spec lists removing Keycloak admin client usage but does not mention: (a) the internal webhook route at `/api/v1/internal/keycloak-webhook` (`/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/cmd/api/main.go` line 728), (b) the `KeycloakWebhookEvent` model type (`/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/model/user.go` lines 34-42), (c) the `HandleKeycloakEvent` method in `UserService` (`/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/service/user.go` line 61). These are Keycloak-specific and should be explicitly listed in the cleanup.

**6. Valkey cache for user lookup needs invalidation strategy**

The spec mentions "Cache this lookup in Valkey with TTL" for the `external_id -> user_id + org_id` mapping but does not specify TTL duration, cache key format, or invalidation strategy (e.g., when user changes org, when user is deleted). Given that the current codebase has no such cache, this should be specified to avoid stale-data bugs.

**7. Missing health check for Zitadel in docker-compose**

The spec's Zitadel service definition has no `healthcheck` block. Both `postgres` and `go-api` have health checks in the current compose file. Since `go-api` will depend on Zitadel for JWKS fetching, add a health check (Zitadel exposes `/debug/healthz`) and update `go-api.depends_on` to use `condition: service_healthy` for Zitadel.

**8. `cloudflared` service needs `network` configuration**

The spec's `cloudflared` service definition does not include `networks: [raven-internal]`. Without this, it cannot reach `zitadel:8080`, `api:8081`, or `frontend:3000` by service name. All other services in the compose file explicitly join `raven-internal`.

**9. Spec references `internal/handler/auth.go` (new callback endpoint) but the file does not exist yet**

The Files Changed Summary lists "Modify: `internal/handler/auth.go`" but this file does not exist in the current codebase. It should say "Create" not "Modify."

**10. Onboarding wizard change from 5-step to 2-step loses LLM provider setup and chatbot test**

The current 5-step wizard (`/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/frontend/src/pages/onboarding/OnboardingWizard.vue`) includes LLM provider configuration (Step 3) and chatbot testing (Step 4). The spec's 2-step wizard drops both. While simplifying onboarding is valid, the spec should note where LLM provider setup moves to (settings page?) since the platform requires it for core functionality.

**11. Token storage "in memory (not localStorage)" needs refresh token strategy**

The spec says to store tokens in memory for security. This means page refresh loses the session. The spec does not address how refresh tokens are handled. `oidc-client-ts` typically uses silent renewal via iframes or service workers. Without specifying this, implementers may re-introduce localStorage or break the UX on refresh.

---

### Summary

The spec's overall architecture is sound. The 8 MUST-FIX items are primarily data model mismatches between the spec and the actual codebase (wrong column names, missing tables, NOT NULL constraint conflicts, port conflicts, and Zitadel config file limitations). These would directly block implementation. The NICE-TO-HAVE items are operational robustness concerns that would cause problems in production but would not prevent initial development.

1	# Zitadel Migration — Design Spec
2	
3	**Date**: 2026-04-13
4	**Status**: Approved
5	**Sub-project**: 1 of 3 (Zitadel migration → Google IDP + WebAuthn → Registration + Org flow)
6	
7	---
8	
9	## Overview
10	
11	Replace Keycloak with Zitadel as the identity provider for Raven. Zitadel handles identity (authentication, Google IDP, passkeys). Raven handles tenancy (org/workspace membership, RLS, billing). This is a full replacement — no dual-auth period.
12	
13	The migration is delivered in **3 phases** (separate PRs):
14	1. Infrastructure — Docker Compose + Zitadel config + Cloudflare Tunnels
15	2. Backend — Go middleware swap + post-signup provisioning endpoints
16	3. Frontend — OIDC client swap + 2-step onboarding wizard + passkey registration
17	
18	## Architecture Decisions
19	
20	| Decision | Choice | Rationale |
21	|----------|--------|-----------|
22	| Multi-tenancy | Single Zitadel org, Raven manages tenancy | Simpler ops, existing RLS model works, no Zitadel API calls for org creation |
23	| First signup | Google OAuth only | Eliminates choice paralysis, fastest onboarding, passkeys added later |
24	| Passkey timing | Post-registration in settings | WebAuthn requires existing account, power-user feature |
25	| Onboarding | 2-step wizard (name org → create KB) | Minimal friction, gets user invested fast |
26	| Deployment | Docker Compose + Cloudflare Tunnels | Same config local and prod, no TLS management, no exposed ports |
27	| Reverse proxy | Cloudflare Tunnels (replaces Traefik) | Zero-config TLS, DDoS protection, no port exposure |
28	
29	## Domain Map
30	
31	| Domain | Service | Internal Port |
32	|--------|---------|---------------|
33	| `auth.ravencloak.org` | Zitadel | 8080 |
34	| `api.ravencloak.org` | Raven Go API | 8081 |
35	| `app.ravencloak.org` | Vue.js frontend | 3000 |
36	| `raven.ravencloak.org` | Landing page | Static (Cloudflare Pages) |
37	
38	---
39	
40	## Phase 1: Infrastructure
41	
42	### Docker Compose Changes
43	
44	**Remove:**
45	- `keycloak` service
46	- Keycloak-specific PostgreSQL database/config
47	- `deploy/keycloak/` directory (realm config, themes)
48	- Traefik service and config (if present)
49	
50	**Add:**

56	  command: >

104	
105	**Zitadel uses Raven's existing PostgreSQL** — separate `zitadel` database on the same instance. The `zitadel` database and user are created automatically by Zitadel's `start-from-init` using the Admin credentials (postgres user with `CREATEDB` privilege). Add `CREATE USER zitadel WITH PASSWORD ... CREATEDB;` to `deploy/postgres/init.sql` as a safety net.
106	
107	**Port conflict resolution**: The Go API must move from port `8080` to `8081` in both `docker-compose.yml` and `internal/config/config.go` (update `v.SetDefault("server.port", 8081)`). Zitadel takes `8080`.
108	
109	**Zitadel config uses env vars, not YAML interpolation**: Zitadel does NOT support `${VAR}` syntax in YAML config files. Instead, use Zitadel's built-in env var mapping — every config key maps to an env var (e.g., `ExternalDomain` → `ZITADEL_EXTERNALDOMAIN`). The `zitadel-config.yaml` should contain only static defaults; dynamic values come from Docker Compose environment variables.
110	
111	**`cloudflared` uses `profiles: [production]`** — only starts when running `docker compose --profile production up`. Local dev accesses services directly on localhost ports.
112

200	
201	---
202	
203	## Phase 2: Backend
204	
205	### JWT Middleware Replacement
206	
207	**Remove:**
208	- `internal/middleware/auth.go` — Keycloak-specific JWT validation
209	- Keycloak realm-based routing logic
210	- `keycloak` Go dependencies
211	
212	**Add:**
213	- New `internal/middleware/auth.go` using `github.com/zitadel/oidc/v3/pkg/op` for token introspection
214	- Or use standard JWT validation with Zitadel's JWKS endpoint: `https://auth.ravencloak.org/.well-known/openid-configuration`
215	- Middleware extracts from JWT: `sub` (Zitadel user ID), `email`, `name`
216	- Sets on Gin context: `userExternalID`, `userEmail`, `userName`
217	
218	**Token validation flow:**
219	1. Extract `Authorization: Bearer <token>` from request header
220	2. Validate JWT signature against Zitadel JWKS (cached)
221	3. Verify `iss` matches Zitadel domain, `aud` matches `raven-app` client ID
222	4. Extract claims, set on context
223	5. Resolve `external_id` → internal `user_id` + `org_id` via database lookup
224	
225	### User Model Changes
226	
227	**Migration:**
228	```sql
229	ALTER TABLE users RENAME COLUMN keycloak_id TO external_id;
230	ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN auth_provider TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'zitadel';
231	CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_users_external_id ON users(external_id);
232	```
233	
234	**Lookup path:**
235	- Request → middleware extracts `sub` from JWT → query `users WHERE external_id = sub` → get `user_id` → query `user_orgs WHERE user_id = ?` → get `org_id`
236	- Cache this lookup in Valkey with TTL (avoid DB hit on every request)
237	
238	### New/Modified Endpoints
239	
240	**`POST /api/v1/auth/callback`** (new, authenticated):
241	- Called by frontend after OIDC callback
242	- Checks if `external_id` exists in `users` table
243	- If new user:
244	  - Creates `users` record (external_id, email, name from JWT claims)
245	  - Returns `{ isNewUser: true }`
246	- If existing user:
247	  - Returns `{ isNewUser: false, orgId: "...", orgSlug: "..." }`
248	
249	**`POST /api/v1/orgs`** (modified):
250	- Remove requirement for `org_admin` role pre-assignment
251	- New rule: if authenticated user has zero orgs (via `user_orgs`), allow org creation
252	- Creates: org record, `user_orgs` entry with `owner` role, free tier subscription via billing service
253	- Returns: `{ orgId, orgSlug }`
254	
255	**Existing endpoints (unchanged, just work with new middleware):**
256	- `POST /api/v1/workspaces` — creates workspace in user's org
257	- `POST /api/v1/knowledge-bases` — creates KB in workspace
258	- `POST /api/v1/documents` — upload documents to KB
259

  "old_string": "### User Model Changes\n\n**Migration:**\n```sql\nALTER TABLE users RENAME COLUMN keycloak_id TO external_id;\nALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN auth_provider TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'zitadel';\nCREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_users_external_id ON users(external_id);\n```\n\n**Lookup path:**\n- Request → middleware extracts `sub` from JWT → query `users WHERE external_id = sub` → get `user_id` → query `user_orgs WHERE user_id = ?` → get `org_id`\n- Cache this lookup in Valkey with TTL (avoid DB hit on every request)\n\n### New/Modified Endpoints\n\n**`POST /api/v1/auth/callback`** (new, authenticated):\n- Called by frontend after OIDC callback\n- Checks if `external_id` exists in `users` table\n- If new user:\n  - Creates `users` record (external_id, email, name from JWT claims)\n  - Returns `{ isNewUser: true }`\n- If existing user:\n  - Returns `{ isNewUser: false, orgId: \"...\", orgSlug: \"...\" }`\n\n**`POST /api/v1/orgs`** (modified):\n- Remove requirement for `org_admin` role pre-assignment\n- New rule: if authenticated user has zero orgs (via `user_orgs`), allow org creation\n- Creates: org record, `user_orgs` entry with `owner` role, free tier subscription via billing service\n- Returns: `{ orgId, orgSlug }`",
  "new_string": "### User Model Changes\n\n**Migration:**\n```sql\n-- Fix: actual column is keycloak_sub, not keycloak_id\nALTER TABLE users RENAME COLUMN keycloak_sub TO external_id;\nALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN auth_provider TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'zitadel';\nDROP INDEX IF EXISTS idx_users_keycloak_sub;\nCREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_users_external_id ON users(external_id);\n\n-- Make org_id nullable for users who have authenticated but not yet created an org\nALTER TABLE users ALTER COLUMN org_id DROP NOT NULL;\n\n-- Drop Keycloak-specific column from organizations\nALTER TABLE organizations DROP COLUMN IF EXISTS keycloak_realm;\n```\n\n**Go model updates:**\n- `internal/model/user.go`: rename `KeycloakSub` field → `ExternalID`, add `AuthProvider` field\n- `internal/model/org.go`: remove `KeycloakRealm` field\n- `internal/repository/user.go`: update all queries referencing `keycloak_sub` → `external_id`\n- `internal/repository/org.go`: update scan to exclude `keycloak_realm`\n\n**Lookup path:**\n- Request → middleware extracts `sub` from JWT → query `users WHERE external_id = sub` → get `user_id` + `org_id`\n- If `org_id IS NULL` → user exists but has no org (needs onboarding)\n- Uses existing `users.org_id` column directly (no `user_orgs` table — it does not exist)\n- Cache: Valkey key `user:{external_id}` → `{user_id, org_id}`, TTL 5 minutes, invalidated on org creation\n\n### New/Modified Endpoints\n\n**`POST /api/v1/auth/callback`** (new — create `internal/handler/auth.go`):\n- Called by frontend after OIDC callback\n- Checks if `external_id` exists in `users` table\n- If new user:\n  - Creates `users` record (external_id, email, name from JWT claims, org_id = NULL)\n  - Returns `{ isNewUser: true }`\n- If existing user with org:\n  - Returns `{ isNewUser: false, orgId: \"...\", orgSlug: \"...\" }`\n- If existing user without org (abandoned onboarding):\n  - Returns `{ isNewUser: true }` (re-enter onboarding)\n\n**`POST /api/v1/orgs`** (modified):\n- Remove `middleware.RequireOrgRole(\"org_admin\")` guard from route in `cmd/api/main.go`\n- New rule: if authenticated user's `org_id IS NULL`, allow org creation\n- Creates: org record, updates `users.org_id` to new org, assigns `owner` role, free tier subscription via billing service\n- Invalidates Valkey user cache\n- Returns: `{ orgId, orgSlug }`\n\n**Middleware context for org-less users:**\n- New middleware helper: `OptionalOrgContext` — sets `ContextKeyOrgID` if user has an org, leaves empty if not\n- Routes that require an org (workspaces, KBs, billing) keep the existing `RequireOrg` middleware\n- Auth callback and org creation routes use `OptionalOrgContext`"
}
```

328	### Login Flow
329	
330	**`/login` route:**
331	- On mount: immediately trigger OIDC redirect with `idp_hint=google` extra param
332	- This makes Zitadel skip its own login UI and redirect straight to Google
333	- User sees: Landing page → Google consent → app callback (no Zitadel UI ever shown)
334	
335	**`/callback` route:**
336	- Exchange authorization code for tokens (PKCE)
337	- Store tokens in memory (not localStorage — security)
338	- Call `POST /api/v1/auth/callback` with the access token
339	- If `isNewUser: true` → navigate to `/onboarding`
340	- If `isNewUser: false` → navigate to `/dashboard`
341	
342	**`/logout`:**
343	- Clear tokens from memory
344	- Redirect to Zitadel end-session endpoint
345	- Zitadel redirects back to landing page
346	
347	### 2-Step Onboarding Wizard
348	
349	**Replaces** the existing 5-step wizard at `frontend/src/pages/onboarding/`.
350	
351	**Step 1: "Name your organization"**
352	- Full-screen centered card
353	- Text input pre-filled with `${userName}'s Team`
354	- Validation: 3-100 chars, alphanumeric + spaces
355	- "Continue" button → `POST /api/v1/orgs` with `{ name: input }`
356	- On success: store `orgId` in state, advance to step 2
357	
358	**Step 2: "Create your first knowledge base"**
359	- KB name input (required, e.g., "Product Docs")
360	- Drag-and-drop file upload zone (optional, accepts PDF/DOCX/MD)
361	- "Get Started" button →
362	  1. `POST /api/v1/workspaces` → creates default workspace
363	  2. `POST /api/v1/knowledge-bases` → creates KB
364	  3. If files uploaded: `POST /api/v1/documents` for each file
365	  4. Navigate to `/dashboard`
366	
367	**Styling**: follows existing frontend patterns (Vue 3 + Tailwind), black/white/amber accent consistent with landing page.
368

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "REDACTED.md",
  "old_string": "**`/login` route:**\n- On mount: immediately trigger OIDC redirect with `idp_hint=google` extra param\n- This makes Zitadel skip its own login UI and redirect straight to Google\n- User sees: Landing page → Google consent → app callback (no Zitadel UI ever shown)\n\n**`/callback` route:**\n- Exchange authorization code for tokens (PKCE)\n- Store tokens in memory (not localStorage — security)\n- Call `POST /api/v1/auth/callback` with the access token",
  "new_string": "**`/login` route:**\n- On mount: immediately trigger OIDC redirect with `idp_hint` extra param\n- **Note**: Zitadel's `idp_hint` value is the IDP's internal Zitadel ID (a numeric/UUID), not the string \"google\". After configuring Google IDP in Zitadel, obtain the IDP ID and store it as `VITE_GOOGLE_IDP_ID` env var.\n- This makes Zitadel skip its own login UI and redirect straight to Google\n- User sees: Landing page → Google consent → app callback (no Zitadel UI ever shown)\n\n**`/callback` route:**\n- Exchange authorization code for tokens (PKCE)\n- Store access token in memory, refresh token in httpOnly cookie (via Go API proxy) or use `oidc-client-ts` silent renewal via iframe\n- **Token refresh strategy**: `oidc-client-ts` `automaticSilentRenew: true` with `silent_redirect_uri` pointing to a minimal `/silent-renew.html` page. This renews tokens before expiry without page reload. If silent renewal fails (e.g., Google session expired), redirect to `/login`.\n- Call `POST /api/v1/auth/callback` with the access token"
}

## KEYCLOAK MIGRATION ANALYSIS — RAVEN REPOSITORY

### 1. **docker-compose.yml** — `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/docker-compose.yml`
- **Keycloak service**: Lines 181-210

Rewrite auth store**\n\nReplace `frontend/src/stores/auth.ts` entirely. The new store uses `oidc-client-ts`:\n\n```typescript\nimport { defineStore } from 'pinia'\nimport { UserManager, WebStorageStateStore } from 'oidc-client-ts'\nimport { ref, computed } from 'vue'\n\nconst userManager = new UserManager({\n  authority: import.meta.env.VITE_ZITADEL_URL,\n  client_id: import.meta.env.VITE_ZITADEL_CLIENT_ID,\n  redirect_uri: `${window.location.origin}/callback`,\n  post_logout_redirect_uri: window.location.origin,\n  silent_redirect_uri: `${window.location.origin}/silent-renew.html`,\n  scope: 'openid profile email',\n  response_type: 'code',\n  automaticSilentRenew: true,\n  userStore: new WebStorageStateStore({ store: window.sessionStorage }),\n})\n\nexport const useAuthStore = defineStore('auth', () => {\n  const user = ref(null)\n  const orgId = ref(null)\n  const isAuthenticated = computed(() => !!user.value)\n  const hasOrg = computed(() => !!orgId.value)\n  const accessToken = computed(() => user.value?.access_token)\n\n  async function init() {\n    const existingUser = await userManager.getUser()\n    if (existingUser && !existingUser.expired) {\n      user.value = existingUser\n    }\n  }\n\n  async function login(idpHint?: string) {\n    const extraParams = idpHint ? { idp_hint: idpHint } : {}\n    await userManager.signinRedirect({ extraQueryParams: extraParams })\n  }\n\n  async function handleCallback() {\n    const callbackUser = await userManager.signinRedirectCallback()\n    user.value = callbackUser\n    return callbackUser\n  }\n\n  async function logout() {\n    await userManager.signoutRedirect()\n    user.value = null\n    orgId.value = null\n  }\n\n  function setOrgId(id: string) {\n    orgId.value = id\n  }\n\n  return { user, orgId, isAuthenticated, hasOrg, accessToken, init, login, handleCallback, logout, setOrgId }\n})\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Update useAuth composable**\n\nUpdate `frontend/src/composables/useAuth.ts` to work with the new store interface.\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Update frontend/.env.example**\n\nRemove `VITE_KEYCLOAK_*` vars.
